## Recommended Architecture

**Core principle: two different sync mechanisms for two different kinds of state, never overlapping.**

1. **Git handles everything version-controlled** (vault content, code). Push/pull is the sync transport for these files — full history, safe merges, no data loss.
2. **Syncthing handles everything git-ignored and machine-local** (`.obsidian/workspace.json`, plugin caches, `.claude/` local session state, Templater cache, `node_modules`-adjacent build artifacts you don't want in git). Syncthing propagates these near-instantly via filesystem watching, with **`.git/` explicitly excluded via `.stignore`**.

### 1. Why not sync `.git` with Syncthing
Syncthing's creator (Jakob Borg) states plainly: git files "are not independent... if you have two sides making concurrent changes Syncthing will 'merge' the two and the result is not something that is consistent in the Git world" — i.e. it corrupts the repo (forum.syncthing.net/t/resolving-sync-conflicts-in-git-folder/11969). The internal git object store, refs, and index are interdependent; Syncthing treats each file as independent and will produce inconsistent states or conflict-copies of `.git/index`, `HEAD`, pack files, etc. **Never sync `.git` directly.** Add `.git` to `.stignore` at the vault root.

### 2. How Syncthing propagation actually works
Folder watching uses `ReadDirectoryChangesW` on Windows and inotify on Linux/WSL — not polling. Real-world propagation is typically **1-10 seconds** for small text files on a LAN (both machines online, direct connection), not literal milliseconds — treat "every millisecond" as "as close to instant as the OS file-watch + hash + transfer pipeline allows." Folder types: use **Send-Receive** (bidirectional, default) for genuinely shared machine-local state; **Receive-Only** is useful for one-way backup mirrors, not relevant here since both laptops are peers. (docs.syncthing.net/users/foldertypes.html)

`.stignore` syntax: patterns are gitignore-like, `**` matches across directories, no trailing slash needed to ignore a directory itself, `!` negates. (docs.syncthing.net/users/ignoring.html)

### 3. Hierarchy for multi-scope sync

- **Layer A — Global machine config** (own Syncthing "folder"): `~/.claude/` (excluding session-transient files), dotfiles, global git config, PowerShell profile. This syncs identically on both machines — a true mirror.
- **Layer B — Jarvis vault** (own Syncthing folder, `.git` ignored): `.obsidian/` local state, plugin data directories not in git, `.claude/` project-local session/scratch files inside the vault. Vault *content* (notes, `CLAUDE.md`, etc.) is committed and pushed via git as normal — do not double-sync it through Syncthing, since two mechanisms writing the same tracked files invites conflict-copy files fighting git's index.
- **Layer C — Per-codebase (second-brain-claudekit, etc.)**: git remotes only, no Syncthing folder needed unless there's git-ignored local state worth mirroring (e.g. `.env.local`, IDE workspace state) — if so, a narrow Syncthing folder scoped to just those files, same pattern as B.

This avoids one giant "sync everything" folder (which would re-invite the Google-Drive-style cache/.json churn problem) in favor of purpose-scoped folders, each with its own `.stignore`.

### 4. Conflict ownership
- **Git-tracked files → git owns conflicts.** Two machines both running Claude Code and committing: normal git merge/rebase conflict resolution applies. Push frequently (a short interval `git pull --rebase &amp;&amp; git push` loop, or a commit-on-save hook) to minimize divergence windows.
- **Syncthing-tracked files → Syncthing's conflict-copy mechanism owns it** (`filename.sync-conflict-&lt;date&gt;-&lt;time&gt;.ext`). This is acceptable for machine-local state like `workspace.json` since conflicts there are cosmetic (which pane was open), not data-loss risks.
- Keeping the two file sets disjoint means a conflict in one system never touches the other.

### 5. Alternatives considered, why Syncthing wins
- **Resilio Sync**: BitTorrent-based, faster for large/many files over WAN, but closed-source core, and the free tier has device/feature limits — Syncthing is free, open, self-hosted, no central tracker dependency.
- **Mutagen**: excellent for one-shot dev-container/remote sync sessions, but designed around a session model (start/stop), not an always-on background daemon between two personal machines — more operational overhead for this use case.
- **rsync + cron/systemd watch**: not real-time (cron) or requires hand-rolling a file-watcher + rsync wrapper that Syncthing already provides robustly, with no built-in bidirectional conflict handling.

### 6. WSL specifics
Access to Windows-drive paths from WSL (`/mnt/c`, `/mnt/d`) goes through the 9P protocol and measured at roughly 6% of native throughput, worse for random/small-file I/O (dev.to/nomurasan writeup, GitHub microsoft/WSL#4197). **Run Syncthing as a native Windows service syncing Windows-path folders** (e.g. `D:\...\Jarvis`), and if WSL needs to read that vault, access it via `/mnt/d/...` for occasional reads — but do heavy file-watching/editing work from the Windows side or keep a second native-ext4 Syncthing instance inside WSL for WSL-only working copies, never both watching the same NTFS folder from two watchers simultaneously.</result>

## Final Report

**1. Existing sync infra is Unison, not Syncthing — and it's same-machine (WSL↔Windows), not cross-laptop.** A manifest-driven system (`second-brain-claudekit/60_Claude/scripts/sync-manifest.json` + `sync-all.sh`) runs on a 15-min Windows Scheduled Task (`ClaudeKit-Sync-All`), syncing `.claude/{agents,commands,hooks}`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md` for 9 "live" projects (second-brain-claudekit, CausalOps, Portfolio, Trading View, Resq, OpsPilot, Jarvis, The Plan, plus curated `.claude_windows`/`.claude_wsl` home-dir mirrors) between WSL repo roots (`~/projects/...`) and this Jarvis vault's `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/&lt;project&gt;/` mirror folders. Requires `-fat` (DrvFs can't hold POSIX permission bits). `.claude_windows`/`.claude_wsl` are deliberately curated — raw `.credentials.json`/`.mcp.json` secrets are permanently excluded, never synced. ## 2. `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/Sync - Unison.md` — full content

215 lines, reproduced above in full in the tool output. Key structural facts your plan needs:
- **Two Unison flag regimes**: WSL↔Windows pairs (crossing DrvFs) always need `-fat`; the doc's original claim that Windows↔Windows (Jarvis-only) doesn't need `-fat` was later *reversed* — the 2026-08-10 postscript says testing showed `-fat` is needed everywhere.
- **Driver**: `60_Claude/scripts/sync-all.sh` reads the manifest, skips non-`live` entries, takes a per-project lock file (`/tmp/claudekit-sync-&lt;name&gt;.lock`), invokes Unison per entry (`-batch -auto -ui text -terse`, `+ -fat` conditionally), appends one line per run to that project's own `Sync-Log.md`, and also writes a combined `_All-Projects-Sync-Log.md` (exists at `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/_All-Projects-Sync-Log.md`, 852KB).
- **Trigger**: one Windows Scheduled Task, `ClaudeKit-Sync-All`, 15-minute interval — the doc's claim "invokes wsl.exe directly, no VBS wrapper" is **stale/wrong**; see item 6 below, current reality uses a VBS wrapper.
- **Naming rule**: mirror folders keep the literal `.claude/` dot-folder name, never renamed for Obsidian visibility.
- **Permanently excluded**: `.claude_windows/`, `.claude_wsl/` raw home-dir dumps are separate, curated, credential-scrubbed mirrors — never point a project sync at them directly.
- Every live project additionally gets a `Setup.md` in its mirror folder, written/maintained only from the Jarvis side, never fed back to source.

**Critical fact for your plan**: this repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`) is **public on GitHub** (per a same-session research finding citing this very `.gitignore`'s own comments) — so anything git already excludes here (`.mcp.json`, `.credentials.json`, `.claude_wsl/`, `.claude_windows/`, PII contact data) is exactly what a Syncthing `.stignore` must *not* substitute for git — those need to stay off any sync path that could leak, or need a genuinely private transport. Beyond that, `.stignore` additionally needs `.git/` itself (Syncthing must never touch git internals — a same-session research fork cites Syncthing's own creator on this causing corruption) plus anything currently gitignored that you *do* still want mirrored machine-to-machine (e.g. `.obsidian/workspace.json`, plugin caches) — those are the exact inverse case: gitignored but still worth Syncthing-ing.

- **The actual current `ClaudeKit-Sync-All` launcher** is `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\30_Order\System\claude-workflow\scripts\sync-all-silent.vbs` (full content, 20 lines):
```vbnet
' Silent launcher for ClaudeKit-Sync-All.
' WindowStyle 0 = hidden -- prevents the every-15-min wsl.exe console popup.
' Correction (2026-08-10): register-sync-task.ps1 originally called wsl.exe
' directly, relying on ScheduledTaskSettingsSet -Hidden to suppress the
' window. That setting hides the task from the Task Scheduler *library* UI,
' not the console window a directly-invoked console executable opens in an
' interactive session -- confirmed for real: the popup kept happening, and
' the window being closed mid-run is the likely cause of LastTaskResult
' 3221225786 (STATUS_CONTROL_C_EXIT) observed 2026-08-10.
' Unlike the retired sync-jarvis-silent.vbs, this launcher WAITS for the
' inner command (waitOnReturn = True) and exits with its real return code,
' so Task Scheduler's LastTaskResult still reflects whether sync-all.sh
' actually succeeded -- closing the exact fire-and-forget gap that let the
' 50_Claude path bug go undetected for over a week (see _docs/Repo-Map.md).
Option Explicit
Dim sh, exitCode
Set sh = CreateObject("WScript.Shell")
exitCode = sh.Run("wsl.exe -e bash -lc ""~/projects/ai/claude/second-brain-claudekit/60_Claude/scripts/sync-all.sh""", 0, True)
WScript.Quit(exitCode)
```
So: **`Sync - Unison.md`'s claim "invokes wsl.exe directly with no VBS wrapper" is now factually wrong** — as of the 2026-08-10 correction documented in the VBS file's own header comment, there *is* a VBS wrapper again, specifically to fix a real console-popup/exit-code bug that direct invocation caused. Flag this drift in your plan if you're relying on `Sync - Unison.md` as ground truth for the trigger mechanism.

# Cross-Laptop Sync: Jarvis + The Plan (Dell ↔ Acer)

## Context

Two laptops (old Dell, this machine — `DESKTOP-3VBG0JH` — and the new Acer) need to converge on one working state. This continues an unresolved 2026-09-16 session (`60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/WSL/Claude Code/anant_gupta/09-16 WSL projects mass commit and sync strategy.md`) that proposed a 3-tier hierarchy and left 4 questions unanswered. Those questions have now been answered this session:

- **Secrets/AI-platform config** (`.claude`, `.cursor`, `.codex`, credentials, SSH keys): stays fresh-install-only per machine, never copied — confirmed, overriding the earlier "exact replication" ask. This matters because Jarvis is a **public** GitHub repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`), so anything secret can never transit through git, and by extension shouldn't transit through the new sync layer either.
- **Syncthing scope**: Jarvis + The Plan vaults (not codebases — those stay GitHub-only for now).
- **Google Drive**: already stopped for both vaults; Syncthing is additive, not a replacement of anything.
- **Discovery**: global discovery + relays (works off-LAN).
- **Git commit cadence**: automatic, scheduled, matching the existing 15-min `ClaudeKit-Sync-All` cadence already running on this machine.
- **new-laptop-windows / new-laptop-wsl**: two new entries in the existing Unison `sync-manifest.json` (same mechanism as the other 9 projects), representing the Acer's Windows-side and WSL-side Claude config, mirrored into `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/`.
- **Browser sync (Vivaldi)**: explicitly out of scope this session.

Three sync mechanisms end up doing three different jobs, deliberately non-overlapping so a conflict in one never touches another:

1. **Git** — owns Jarvis's version history and GitHub backup. Auto-committed on a schedule, pushed with rebase+retry.
2. **Syncthing** — owns real-time propagation of the *live working tree* (including everything git ignores that isn't secret: `.obsidian/workspace.json`, plugin caches, etc.), between Dell and Acer. Never touches `.git/` internals directly — Syncthing's own maintainer documents that concurrent edits to git's internal files produce inconsistent repo state, not a working merge.
3. **Unison (existing, unchanged)** — owns same-machine WSL↔Windows mirroring of `.claude` config into Jarvis's `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/<project>/` folders. Cross-machine propagation of *that* content happens for free once Syncthing carries the vault, so no change to Unison's own scope is needed — just two new manifest entries.

Everything below is scoped to what's executable **from this Dell session now**, versus what becomes a documented runbook for the Acer (I have no tool access to that physical machine).

## What gets built (Dell-side, executed this session)

**1. Git workflow for this infra change itself**
- New branch `infra/cross-laptop-sync` off `master`.
- All new files/config below committed there; merge to `master` once verified (this repo has always committed straight to `master` for ongoing vault work — no change to that pattern going forward, only this build gets its own branch).

**2. Syncthing install + config (Dell)**
- Install via `winget install Syncthing.Syncthing`.
- Two folders added to Syncthing: Jarvis vault (`D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`) and The Plan vault, both Send-Receive, global discovery + relays on (default).
- New file `.stignore` at Jarvis vault root (committed to git, so both machines' Syncthing read the same rules):
  ```
  .git
  .mcp.json
  .credentials.json
  .env
  .env.local
  .claude/.env.local
  .claude/settings.local.json
  30_Order/System/jarvis-memory/*.sqlite*
  60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/**/_raw_jsonl
  60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/**/_raw_composer
  20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/.claude_windows
  20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/.claude_wsl
  20_Progress/AI/Cursor/.cursor_windows
  20_Progress/AI/Cursor/.cursor_wsl
  20_Progress/AI/Kiro/.kiro_windows
  20_Progress/AI/Kiro/.kiro_wsl
  ```
  This mirrors `.gitignore`'s secret/generated-noise entries (never leave the machine, by any channel) while letting everything else git already ignores — Obsidian workspace state, plugin caches — ride Syncthing instead. The raw-AI-transcript and curated `.claude_*`/`.cursor_*`/`.kiro_*` home-dir mirrors are excluded by default as a conservative call since they were flagged "never commit" for being unredacted/raw; easy to remove from `.stignore` later if you want them mirrored too.
- The Plan vault gets an equivalent `.stignore`, adjusted for its own gitignore once I read it (same principle).

**3. Automatic git commit/push for Jarvis**
- New script `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/git-auto-sync.ps1`: takes a lock file (same per-name-lock pattern the existing Unison driver already uses), `git pull --rebase`, commits if there's a diff, pushes with one retry-on-rejection (pull --rebase, push again) to handle the case where both laptops' scheduled task fire close together.
- New Scheduled Task `Jarvis-GitAutoSync`, 15-min interval (matching `ClaudeKit-Sync-All`), `powershell.exe -WindowStyle Hidden -File git-auto-sync.ps1` — no VBS wrapper needed here since it's a native PowerShell target, not a console app like `wsl.exe` (that's what forced the VBS wrapper for the Unison launcher).
- Same script + task gets documented for the Acer (it's independent per-machine; each laptop commits/pushes its own local state).

**4. new-laptop-windows / new-laptop-wsl**
- Edit `~/projects/ai/claude/second-brain-claudekit/60_Claude/scripts/sync-manifest.json` (reachable from here via `wsl -d Ubuntu -- ...`, since that repo's canonical copy lives in WSL on this Dell) to add two entries, `status: "candidate"` until verified live on the Acer:
  ```json
  { "name": "new-laptop-windows", "status": "candidate", "repo_root": "<Acer Windows home .claude>", "mirror_path": ".../Jarvis/20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/new-laptop-windows", "paths": [".claude/agents", ".claude/commands", ".claude/hooks", "CLAUDE.md"], "needs_fat": true }
  { "name": "new-laptop-wsl", "status": "candidate", "repo_root": "<Acer WSL home .claude>", "mirror_path": ".../Jarvis/20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/new-laptop-wsl", "paths": [".claude/agents", ".claude/commands", ".claude/hooks", "CLAUDE.md"], "needs_fat": true }
  ```
  (`settings.local.json`/`.mcp.json` deliberately excluded from `paths`, matching every existing entry.)
- Commit + push this manifest change to `second-brain-claudekit` on GitHub (Tier 1: code syncs via git, not Unison, not Syncthing).
- Create `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/new-laptop-windows/{Setup.md,Sync-Log.md}` and the `-wsl` sibling in Jarvis, matching the existing per-project convention (e.g. `second-brain-claudekit/Setup.md`).

**5. Architecture doc**
- New note `40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync — Syncthing Architecture.md` (standard `type: concept` frontmatter) recording this decision, superseding the 09-16 session's open questions, and giving the exact Acer-side runbook (install Syncthing, exchange Device IDs, accept the two vault folders, clone `second-brain-claudekit`, flip the two manifest entries to `status: "live"` once its own scheduled task runs clean once).
- Small factual patch to `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/Sync - Unison.md`: it currently claims "invokes wsl.exe directly, no VBS wrapper," which the 2026-08-10 header comment in `sync-all-silent.vbs` itself contradicts (a wrapper was re-added to fix a console-popup/exit-code bug). One-paragraph correction under the existing heading, not a rewrite.

Git workflow for this infra change itself**\n- New branch `infra/cross-laptop-sync` off `master`.\n- All new files/config below committed there; merge to `master` once verified (this repo has always committed straight to `master` for ongoing vault work — no change to that pattern going forward, only this build gets its own branch).\n\n**2. Syncthing install + config (Dell)**\n- Install via `winget install Syncthing.Syncthing`.\n- Two folders added to Syncthing: Jarvis vault (`D:\\Users\\_Anant\\10_Areas\\Documents\\Jarvis`) and The Plan vault, both Send-Receive, global discovery + relays on (default).\n- New file `.stignore` at Jarvis vault root (committed to git, so both machines\u0027 Syncthing read the same rules):\n  ```\n  .git\n  .mcp.json\n  .credentials.json\n  .env\n  .env.local\n  .claude/.env.local\n  .claude/settings.local.json\n  30_Order/System/jarvis-memory/*.sqlite*\n  60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/**/_raw_jsonl\n  60_Claude/05_Clippings/AI Conversations/**/_raw_composer\n  20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/.claude_windows\n  20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/.claude_wsl\n  20_Progress/AI/Cursor/.cursor_windows\n  20_Progress/AI/Cursor/.cursor_wsl\n  20_Progress/AI/Kiro/.kiro_windows\n  20_Progress/AI/Kiro/.kiro_wsl\n  ```\n  This mirrors `.gitignore`\u0027s secret/generated-noise entries (never leave the machine, by any channel) while letting everything else git already ignores — Obsidian workspace state, plugin caches — ride Syncthing instead. The raw-AI-transcript and curated `.claude_*`/`.cursor_*`/`.kiro_*` home-dir mirrors are excluded by default as a conservative call since they were flagged \"never commit\" for being unredacted/raw; easy to remove from `.stignore` later if you want them mirrored too.\n- The Plan vault gets an equivalent `.stignore`, adjusted for its own gitignore once I read it (same principle).\n\n**3. Automatic git commit/push for Jarvis**\n- New script `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/git-auto-sync.ps1`: takes a lock file (same per-name-lock pattern the existing Unison driver already uses), `git pull --rebase`, commits if there\u0027s a diff, pushes with one retry-on-rejection (pull --rebase, push again) to handle the case where both laptops\u0027 scheduled task fire close together.\n- New Scheduled Task `Jarvis-GitAutoSync`, 15-min interval (matching `ClaudeKit-Sync-All`), `powershell.exe -WindowStyle Hidden -File git-auto-sync.ps1` — no VBS wrapper needed here since it\u0027s a native PowerShell target, not a console app like `wsl.exe` (that\u0027s what forced the VBS wrapper for the Unison launcher).\n- Same script + task gets documented for the Acer (it\u0027s independent per-machine; each laptop commits/pushes its own local state).\n\n**4. new-laptop-windows / new-laptop-wsl**\n- Edit `~/projects/ai/claude/second-brain-claudekit/60_Claude/scripts/sync-manifest.json` (reachable from here via `wsl -d Ubuntu -- ...`, since that repo\u0027s canonical copy lives in WSL on this Dell) to add two entries, `status: \"candidate\"` until verified live on the Acer:\n  ```json\n  { \"name\": \"new-laptop-windows\", \"status\": \"candidate\", \"repo_root\": \"\u003cAcer Windows home .claude\u003e\", \"mirror_path\": \".../Jarvis/20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/new-laptop-windows\", \"paths\": [\".claude/agents\", \".claude/commands\", \".claude/hooks\", \"CLAUDE.md\"], \"needs_fat\": true }\n  { \"name\": \"new-laptop-wsl\", \"status\": \"candidate\", \"repo_root\": \"\u003cAcer WSL home .claude\u003e\", \"mirror_path\": \".../Jarvis/20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/new-laptop-wsl\", \"paths\": [\".claude/agents\", \".claude/commands\", \".claude/hooks\", \"CLAUDE.md\"], \"needs_fat\": true }\n  ```\n  (`settings.local.json`/`.mcp.json` deliberately excluded from `paths`, matching every existing entry.)\n- Commit + push this manifest change to `second-brain-claudekit` on GitHub (Tier 1: code syncs via git, not Unison, not Syncthing).\n- Create `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/new-laptop-windows/{Setup.md,Sync-Log.md}` and the `-wsl` sibling in Jarvis, matching the existing per-project convention (e.g. `second-brain-claudekit/Setup.md`).\n\n**5. # Cross-Laptop Sync — Build 0 only: Roadmap + Pre-Mortem Handoff

## Context

The full architecture from the prior draft of this plan (Syncthing for Jarvis + The Plan vaults, scheduled git auto-commit, new Unison manifest entries for the Acer) is not being executed in this session. The user explicitly rejected running it directly: this sync process has to "take place once and never be repaired again," the current research is judged insufficient to guarantee that ("bound to break for sure"), and the user wants small, individually-verifiable builds — each executed by a **separate, freshly-started Claude Code session inside Jarvis**, not this planning session. The user is installing Syncthing themselves and will decide the next concrete step based on how it behaves.

So this round's deliverable is not the sync system — it's the **first handoff**: a roadmap note mapping out the small builds, and one fully self-contained prompt note for Build 0 (a pre-mortem on the whole design) that a fresh session with zero conversation memory can pick up and execute correctly.

## What gets written this round

**1. `40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync — Build Roadmap.md`** (type: `action`, per the vault's Action Standard — link-dense, statements not checkboxes)
Lists the builds in order, each a separate future session:
- Build 0 — Pre-mortem on the Syncthing + git + Unison design (this round's other deliverable)
- Build 1 — Syncthing installed + Jarvis vault as a single-machine pilot folder on the Dell only (no Acer pairing yet), `.stignore` correctness verified
- Build 2 — Acer-side Syncthing install + device pairing + first real two-machine sync, smallest possible slice
- Build 3 — Scheduled git auto-commit/push for Jarvis (independent of Syncthing, testable alone)
- Build 4 — The Plan vault added as a second Syncthing folder
- Build 5 — `new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl` Unison manifest entries + Acer-side wiring
- Build 6 — general "everything else" folder (blocked on the user naming concrete target paths)

Each entry states its own done-condition and what it depends on, so a session can be pointed at "Build 2" alone and know whether it's unblocked yet.

**2. `40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync — Build 0 Pre-Mortem Prompt.md`**
A self-contained brief (written so it needs no other context) instructing a fresh session to stress-test the proposed design before anything beyond Syncthing itself gets installed. It must cover, concretely:
- Syncthing + git in the same working tree: confirm `.stignore` fully protects `.git/`, and what happens if a `git pull --rebase` runs while Syncthing is mid-transfer into the same folder.
- Obsidian file-locking behavior when a note is open on one laptop while Syncthing writes an incoming change to it.
- Laptop sleep/wake and lid-close behavior — does Syncthing resume cleanly, and what's the propagation gap while one machine was offline.
- Conflict-copy storms: what actually happens if both laptops edit the same note within the same few seconds, whether that's likely given normal use, and what the recovery process looks like.
- `.stignore` correctness against the exact current `.gitignore` (secrets must never leave the machine via either channel) and against the curated `.claude_windows`/`.claude_wsl`/etc. mirrors this session defaulted to excluding — confirm or revise that call.
- Global discovery/relay privacy tradeoffs in concrete terms (what a relay operator can see) given this is a personal vault.
- Version/update behavior — what happens if the two laptops end up on different Syncthing versions, and what update discipline avoids that.
- A concrete rollback plan if something goes wrong mid-sync (how to safely disable a folder without data loss).
Output: a findings note the user reviews before approving Build 1 — not code, not config changes.

Both notes get written directly (Write tool), following the vault's frontmatter conventions and `HUMAN_WRITING.md` voice — no execution, no installs, no branch, no commits, nothing outside these two notes.

## Verification
- Both notes exist at the paths above, valid frontmatter, and the Build 0 note is genuinely self-contained (no unexplained references to "this session" or "the earlier plan").
- Nothing else in the repo changes — `git status` after this round shows only these two new files.

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap
## One-Line Answer
**Syncthing** carries the live **Jarvis** and **The Plan** vaults between the Dell and the Acer in near-real-time, **Git** keeps Jarvis's version history and GitHub backup on its own schedule, and the existing same-machine **Unison** mirror gets two new entries for the Acer's Claude config. This note is the execution index — each build below is its own session, and nothing runs until the build before it is verified.
## Why small builds instead of one setup
This has to run correctly once and never need repair — that's a much higher bar than a normal feature build. A single session drafted the full design (Syncthing + scheduled Git + two new Unison entries) on 2026-09-18 and the plan itself looked sound, but "looked sound to one session's research pass" is not the same as "safe to run unattended forever on two machines." The decision was to stop before executing it, split it into builds small enough to verify individually, and run each one in its own fresh Claude Code session with a self-contained prompt — so no build inherits an assumption nobody checked.
## Decisions already locked in (2026-09-18)
Do not re-litigate these in a later build session — they were asked directly and answered:
- **Secrets** (`.claude`, `.cursor`, `.codex`, credentials, SSH keys) stay fresh-install-only per machine. Never copied, never synced, by any mechanism — this overrides an earlier "exact replication" request once the tension was surfaced. Jarvis is a **public** GitHub repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`), which is part of why this matters.
- **Syncthing scope**: the Jarvis and The Plan vaults. Not codebases — those stay GitHub-only for now.
- **Google Drive**: already stopped syncing both vaults (too much cache/`.json` churn). Syncthing is additive, not a replacement of anything Drive still does elsewhere.
- **Discovery mode**: global discovery + relays, so sync works when the two laptops aren't on the same network.
- **Git commit cadence for Jarvis**: automatic, scheduled — not manual-trigger.
- **`new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl`**: two new entries in the existing Unison `sync-manifest.json` (same shape as the other 9 project entries), representing the Acer's Windows-side and WSL-side Claude config.
- **Browser sync (Vivaldi)**: explicitly out of scope. Syncing a live browser profile folder via Syncthing risks corrupting its SQLite session/history database while both browsers are running — use Vivaldi's own built-in account sync instead, whenever that gets picked up.
## Build order
### Build 0 - Pre-mortem on the design
*Depends on:* nothing. Read-only research and analysis.
*Done when:* a findings note exists naming every real failure mode in the Syncthing + Git + Unison design, with a verdict on whether Build 1 is safe to start.
*Prompt:* [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 0 Pre-Mortem Prompt]].
### Build 1 - Syncthing, Dell-only pilot
*Depends on:* Build 0 coming back clean (or its fixes applied). Syncthing installed on the Dell — the user is doing this step manually.
*Done when:* the Jarvis vault runs as a single Syncthing folder on the Dell alone, `.stignore` is verified to keep `.git/` and every secret path out, and nothing about normal vault use (Obsidian open, git commits, Claude Code sessions) breaks with Syncthing watching the folder.
*Not in scope:* pairing with the Acer. This build proves the folder is safe to watch before a second machine touches it.
### Build 2 - Acer pairing, first real two-machine sync
*Depends on:* Build 1 verified clean, Syncthing installed on the Acer.
*Done when:* the two laptops exchange Device IDs, the Jarvis folder syncs one real edit end to end, and a deliberate simultaneous-edit test on both machines produces the expected conflict-copy behavior (not data loss).
### Build 3 - Scheduled Git auto-commit/push
*Depends on:* nothing from Builds 1-2 — this is independent and can run in parallel with them. Touches Jarvis's git history and a new Scheduled Task, so it needs its own careful build regardless.
*Done when:* a script commits and pushes Jarvis on a schedule, survives a forced push-conflict (both laptops' tasks firing close together) with a rebase-and-retry, and a manual dry run shows a clean exit with nothing staged when there's nothing to commit.
### Build 4 - The Plan vault added
*Depends on:* Build 2 verified stable for at least a few days of normal use.
*Done when:* The Plan vault syncs the same way Jarvis does, with its own `.stignore` built from its own gitignore.
### Build 5 - `new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl` wiring
*Depends on:* Build 2 (the two laptops need to already share the vault, since the Unison mirror folders these entries write into live inside it). `second-brain-claudekit` cloned on the Acer.
*Done when:* both manifest entries run clean on the Acer at least once and flip from `candidate` to `live`, matching the other 9 project entries' convention.
### Build 6 - General folder sync
*Depends on:* the user naming concrete target folders. Not started — guessing at paths here risks recreating the exact Google Drive cache/churn problem that already got ruled out once.
## Open items
- Which specific folders "everything else" actually means — apps sync themselves already, so this is about plain files/documents outside the two vaults. Needs a real list before Build 6 gets scoped.
- Whether Build 0's session can execute anything (e.g. a local two-folder Syncthing test on one machine to probe conflict behavior) or should stay pure research — the Build 0 prompt itself sets that boundary explicitly.

```
- `Write` -- `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\40_Resources\CS\Concepts\New Laptop\Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 0 Pre-Mortem Prompt.md`
```

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 0 Pre-Mortem Prompt
## What you're doing
Stress-test a proposed cross-laptop sync design before anything beyond **Syncthing** itself gets installed. This is research and analysis, not implementation — the output is a findings note, not code or config changes. Read this whole note first; it's written to need nothing else.
## Context (self-contained)
Two laptops need to converge on one working state: an old Dell (`DESKTOP-3VBG0JH`) and a new Acer, both running Windows 11 with WSL2 Ubuntu. The vault is `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`, a **public** GitHub repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`) — nothing secret can ever transit through it or through any sync mechanism layered on top of it. A sibling vault, The Plan, follows the same pattern.
The proposed design has three layers that are meant to never overlap, so a conflict in one never touches another:
1. **Git** owns Jarvis's version history and GitHub backup, committed and pushed on an automatic schedule (not yet built).
2. **Syncthing** owns real-time propagation of the live working tree between the two laptops — everything git ignores that isn't secret (`.obsidian/workspace.json`, plugin caches, etc.) rides this layer, since git never carries it. Syncthing must never touch `.git/` internals directly — its own maintainer has stated that two machines concurrently writing into git's internal object store produces inconsistent repo state, not a working merge, so the plan excludes `.git/` via `.stignore`.
3. **Unison** (already exists, unchanged in scope) mirrors WSL-side Claude Code config into Windows-side Jarvis folders on the *same* machine, 15-minute Scheduled Task, driven by a manifest at `~/projects/ai/claude/second-brain-claudekit/60_Claude/scripts/sync-manifest.json`. It has nothing to do with cross-laptop sync directly — but once Syncthing carries the vault, whatever Unison writes into the vault on one machine propagates to the other for free.
Decisions already locked in, not open for re-debate here: secrets/AI-platform config stay fresh-install-only per machine, never synced by anything; Syncthing scope is the two vaults only, not codebases; Google Drive already stopped syncing both vaults; discovery mode is global (not LAN-only); Git auto-commit is scheduled, not manual.
Full context: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap]].
## What to answer
Each of these needs a concrete, mechanism-level answer — not "this should be fine."
### Syncthing and Git sharing a working tree
Confirm `.stignore` actually protects `.git/` completely — check Syncthing's own documentation and forum guidance on this exact failure mode. Then work out what happens if a scheduled `git pull --rebase` runs on one machine at the exact moment Syncthing is mid-transfer of a file into that same folder — does git see a half-written file, and if so, under what condition.
### Obsidian file locks
What happens when a note is open and being edited in Obsidian on one laptop while Syncthing writes an incoming change to that same file from the other laptop. Does Obsidian reload cleanly, silently overwrite the open buffer, or produce a Syncthing conflict-copy — and does the answer differ for `.md` notes versus `.obsidian/*.json` plugin state files that are open/locked by a running Obsidian process.
### Sleep, wake, and offline gaps
Does Syncthing resume cleanly after a laptop sleeps or the lid closes for hours, and what's the realistic propagation gap for changes made on the other machine while one was offline. Does anything about Windows' sleep/hibernate behavior specifically break a background Syncthing service.
### Conflict-copy storms
Work out concretely what happens if both laptops edit the same note within the same few seconds — how likely is that given actual daily use (one person, two laptops, not usually both open and being typed into at once) versus scheduled processes (the Git auto-commit and the Unison sync) touching files at the same moment as a Syncthing transfer. Describe the actual recovery process for a `sync-conflict` file: does it need manual resolution every time, or only in the rare true-double-edit case.
### `.stignore` correctness
The draft `.stignore` excludes `.git`, every secret path from the vault's `.gitignore` (`.mcp.json`, `.credentials.json`, `.env*`, `.claude/settings.local.json`), and — as a conservative default — the curated `.claude_windows`/`.claude_wsl`/`.cursor_windows`/`.cursor_wsl`/`.kiro_windows`/`.kiro_wsl` home-directory mirrors and the raw AI-conversation transcript dumps (`_raw_jsonl`, `_raw_composer`), since those were flagged "never commit" in git for being unredacted. Confirm this list is actually complete against the vault's real current `.gitignore`, and give a verdict on whether the curated-mirror exclusion is the right call or overly cautious — those are described elsewhere as already credential-scrubbed.
### Global discovery and relay privacy
State concretely what a Syncthing relay operator can and can't see when relaying encrypted traffic between the two laptops (file names, sizes, folder structure, contents) — cite Syncthing's own security documentation rather than assuming.
### Version drift
What happens if the two laptops end up running different Syncthing versions for a while, and what update discipline avoids protocol-incompatibility or subtle sync-behavior differences.
### Rollback
Give a concrete, tested-in-documentation procedure for safely disabling a Syncthing folder on one or both machines without losing data or leaving the vault in a half-synced state — this needs to exist before Build 1 starts, not be improvised if something goes wrong mid-build.
## Output
Write findings to a new note in this same folder: `Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 0 Findings.md`, same frontmatter shape as this note, linked back to both this prompt and the roadmap. End with an explicit verdict: which of the above are real risks that need a design change before Build 1, which are acceptable as-is, and which need a live test (not just documentation) to actually confirm.
## Do not
- Do not pair with the Acer or touch any device outside this machine.
- Do not edit `sync-manifest.json`, `.gitignore`, or any existing vault note beyond writing the findings note.
- Do not create a Scheduled Task, install anything beyond Syncthing itself if it helps verify a specific claim locally, or touch git branches/history.
- If a genuinely necessary local test would require any of the above, name it as a recommendation in the findings note instead of running it.

---
name: feedback-highstakes-infra-small-builds
description: For must-never-break infrastructure (sync daemons, cross-machine automation), don't execute a drafted plan directly - split into small verifiable builds handed off to separate fresh sessions, with a pre-mortem before the first real build

Rule: when the user describes infra work as needing to "take place once and never be repaired again" (e.g. cross-laptop Syncthing sync, always-on scheduled automation), do not execute a freshly-drafted plan in the same session that designed it - even after research and clarifying questions made the plan look sound. Split it into small, individually-verifiable builds. The planning/research session writes detailed, self-contained context notes and handoff prompts into the vault; each build then runs in its own fresh Claude Code session, one at a time, with no execution happening in the planning session itself. Before the first build that touches an unfamiliar tool/mechanism, insert a dedicated pre-mortem/failure-mode-analysis build (read-only, output is a findings note) rather than going straight to implementation.

Why: confirmed 2026-09-18 during [[project_jarvis_cross_laptop_sync]] planning. After a full architecture was researched (via parallel forks), clarified (via AskUserQuestion), and written up as an approved plan, the user rejected direct execution: "This is a solid plan but it is a lot of work to be completed in one go... this is bound to break for sure [with the knowledge you currently have]... The plan needs extreme improvements and needs to be executed in small builds... You will not be executing anything in this session. You will be providing prompts for another session inside jarvis to do the work." The concern was specifically that one session's research pass, however thorough, isn't the same bar as "safe to run unattended forever on two machines" - the reliability requirement is categorically higher than a normal feature build.

How to apply: when a request is architecturally large AND explicitly framed as permanent/high-reliability/hard-to-fix-later (sync systems, backup automation, scheduled jobs touching git or multiple machines, anything the user calls "set and forget"), default to: (1) research and produce a full plan as usual, (2) before executing, ask whether they want it run now or handed off in stages, (3) if staged, write a roadmap note breaking it into builds with explicit depends-on/done-when conditions, and (4) write the first build (usually a pre-mortem) as a fully self-contained prompt note a context-free session could execute correctly. Don't assume this staged approach for ordinary feature work or reversible changes - it's specifically for the "must never break, must never need repair" class of request.

Good, that closes real gaps in what I told you earlier. Let me answer each question directly, then give you the full postmortem.

## Is Syncthing actually the right call?

Yes, and it's worth re-confirming why now that you're pushing on it. Comparing it fresh against Resilio Sync and Nextcloud:

- **Resilio Sync** uses BitTorrent-style chunked transfer, which is genuinely faster for large files. But it's closed-source with a proprietary protocol, and the free tier is feature-gated. For a vault that's mostly small text files, its speed advantage doesn't matter, and "closed-source tool I depend on forever" is a worse fit for "set up once, never touch again."
- **Nextcloud** isn't peer-to-peer at all, it's a personal cloud server both laptops sync to. That means you'd be running and maintaining a server, which is the opposite of "never repaired again" - it's one more thing that can go down.
- **Syncthing** is open-source, peer-to-peer (no central server, no account), and free. It wins specifically on the properties you said matter: nothing to host, nothing to pay for, nothing to trust a vendor with.

So yes, Syncthing remains the right tool. Not because it's the only option, but because the alternatives trade away exactly the properties you're optimizing for.

## Will it sync codebases too?

No, and this is worth correcting directly since you flagged it as an assumption. The locked decision from earlier this session is Syncthing covers only the two Obsidian vaults. Codebases stay GitHub-only, push and pull.

That's not an arbitrary line. I just pulled the Syncthing community's own experience with this: syncing a live git working tree with a file-sync tool is a known corruption path. From the Syncthing forum: when two sides make concurrent changes, Syncthing "merges" them at the file level, and the result "is not something that is consistent in the Git world." Another user reported their repository was destroyed this way, only recoverable because the GitHub remote still had good history. The underlying reason: git's `.git/` directory is not a set of independent files, it's an interdependent object store, index, and ref set, and "lots of small files changing often tend to confuse file synchronization tools." Code repos change constantly in exactly that pattern. Git already gives you a real sync mechanism for code, versioned, with actual merge logic, so there's no reason to double up.

The Jarvis vault itself has a `.git/` folder too, since it's also a git repo. Same rule applies there: `.git/` gets excluded from Syncthing via `.stignore`, for the identical reason.

## Is the "one Syncthing instance for everything" call still right?

Yes, that's what you picked when I asked, and it still holds up: one daemon, one thing to monitor, one update cadence, one log to check. Running a second sync product for "everything else" on top of Syncthing-for-vaults would mean two independent failure surfaces instead of one, which cuts against the reliability bar you're setting. If you want to revisit it, the alternative is a second tool for non-vault folders, but nothing in this conversation gives a reason to.

## How Syncthing actually works, mechanically

**A "folder" is a literal directory you point it at, not a name match.** In Syncthing's config you add a Folder ID (an arbitrary label) and a Folder Path (a real path on disk, e.g. `D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`). Everything under that path syncs recursively, except what `.stignore` excludes. It has no idea what's "in" the folder until it scans it - it's not matching by folder name anywhere else on the system. Jarvis and The Plan would be two separate Folder entries, independently configured, independently ignorable.

**Change detection is two-layered.** An OS-level file watcher (ReadDirectoryChangesW on Windows, inotify on Linux/WSL) catches changes essentially immediately. A periodic full rescan runs on top as a safety net, in case the watcher missed something (OS notification limits, the process being suspended, etc.) - so "real-time" here means "as fast as the OS watcher fires," not literal milliseconds, with the rescan as a backstop, not the primary mechanism.

**Transfer is block-level, not whole-file.** Files get split into blocks (128 KiB to 16 MiB depending on file size, capped so no file has more than ~2000 blocks). Each block gets hashed. Devices exchange an index of file metadata, a version vector per file plus a sequence number, not file content, over an encrypted, mutually authenticated connection between device IDs (which are public-key fingerprints). When a peer's version vector for a file is newer, the local device requests only the blocks it's missing, and can even reuse an identical block from a completely different file if the hash matches. So editing one line of a 50 KB note does not re-transfer the whole 50 KB.

**Discovery and relay:** direct LAN connection when possible, global discovery servers to find a peer's current IP when it's not, and relay servers as a fallback when a direct connection can't be established (both machines behind NAT, different networks). I checked what a relay operator can actually see: your device ID, your IP, and the volume of traffic. Content stays end-to-end encrypted through the relay, "much like a router." File names and file content are not exposed to relay operators.

**Versioning is off by default.** Left unconfigured, a changed or deleted file is just overwritten or removed, no history. Turning on Staggered versioning keeps periodic snapshots in a hidden `.stversions` folder (roughly: every 30 seconds for the first hour, hourly for the first day, daily for 30 days, weekly after that) - genuinely worth turning on for both vaults as an "oops" safety net, since it's the closest thing Syncthing has to undo.

**Conflicts don't merge content.** If two devices both change the same file since the last version both agreed on, Syncthing can't determine which is "right," so it doesn't try to merge them. It keeps one as the real filename and renames the other to `filename.sync-conflict-<date>-<time>-<deviceID>.ext`. No data is silently lost, but you get two files and have to manually reconcile. This only fires on genuine concurrent edits, not on one device editing while the other is idle.

## Full postmortem on the build plan as it stands

**Confirmed real risk, already mitigated in the plan:** Syncthing syncing `.git/` internals directly. This is a documented corruption path (see above), and the plan already excludes `.git` via `.stignore`. This only holds if the exclusion is verified correct in Build 1, not assumed.

**Confirmed real risk, structurally avoided by the plan:** Syncthing syncing codebases at all. Already scoped out. If this ever gets revisited, the corruption risk above is the reason not to, not a preference.

**Genuinely unresolved, needs a live test, not just docs:** Obsidian's behavior when a note is open and being edited on one laptop while Syncthing writes an incoming change to that same file. I could not find Syncthing or Obsidian documentation that addresses this specific interaction. The plausible failure mode: Syncthing writes atomically (temp file plus rename), so the file on disk is never half-written, but if Obsidian has that file open with unsaved keystrokes in its buffer and the file changes underneath it, the outcome (silent reload, conflict prompt, or Obsidian's autosave overwriting the incoming change a few seconds later) is not something I can state with confidence from documentation. This has to be tested for real in Build 1, on the Dell alone, before Build 2 puts a second machine in the loop.

**Real but bounded risk:** conflict-copy storms. Given actual usage (one person, generally on one laptop at a time), true concurrent edits of the same note are unlikely, but two automated processes can collide: the scheduled Git auto-commit (Build 3) and a Syncthing transfer landing on the same file within the same window. This is a real interaction between two of your own builds, not just a Syncthing quirk, and it's part of why Build 3 needs its own pull-rebase-retry logic rather than a bare commit-and-push.

**Low risk, now confirmed:** relay privacy. Content is end-to-end encrypted through a relay; the relay only sees device IDs, IP addresses, and traffic volume, not file names or content. Global discovery + relays is fine for a personal vault.

**Real but manageable risk:** version drift between the two laptops' Syncthing installs. Mitigated by picking one update discipline (e.g., update both within the same week whenever either updates) and stating it explicitly rather than letting it drift silently.

**Open risk with no mitigation yet:** rollback. There's no tested "disable this folder safely without losing data" procedure written down yet. This needs to exist before Build 1 starts, not get improvised if something breaks mid-sync.

**Scope-creep risk:** "sync everything" for the whole-machine layer. This is exactly how the Google Drive problem happened once already. The plan currently blocks Build 6 on you naming concrete folders for exactly this reason, and that block should stay until you do.

## On `.obsidian`: yes, this is straightforward

Exclude (matches what's already gitignored, for the same reasons: secrets or pure churn):
`.obsidian/workspace.json`, `.obsidian/workspace-mobile.json`, `.trash/`, `.obsidian/graph.json`, `.obsidian/workspaces.json`, `.obsidian/plugins/copilot/data.json`, `.obsidian/plugins/quickadd/data.json`, `.obsidian/plugins/obsidian-local-rest-api/data.json` (these three hold API keys), `.obsidian/copilot-index-*.json` (large, regenerable).

Include (this is the actual point of adding Syncthing on top of git: these are settings you want identical on both laptops, and git never carries them):
`.obsidian/app.json`, `appearance.json`, `hotkeys.json`, `community-plugins.json`, `core-plugins.json`, snippets, themes, and the non-secret entries under `.obsidian/plugins/*/data.json`.

This confirms the `.stignore` list from the roadmap note is the right shape, it just needed the "why" spelled out per-file rather than asserted.

## Main folders (Google Drive)

Your proposed setup is the right mechanism, and it has real names: Google Drive for Desktop's **Mirror** mode (full local copy) versus **Stream** mode (files stay cloud-only, cached on open). Dell on Mirror, Acer on Stream, consolidated to one account, does solve the storage/duplication problem for plain document folders, and it's a different mechanism from Syncthing on purpose, since those folders aren't git repos and don't need real-time bidirectional sync, just "available on both machines." One open item I can't resolve for you: which of the two current Google accounts becomes the single surviving one. That only matters once we get to that build, so I've logged it as an open item rather than blocking on it now.

Desktop-folder sync (shortcuts) is a separate, smaller Drive feature (back up the Desktop folder) and fits the same build.

## Codebases

Confirmed, not re-decided: deferred until after Jarvis is fully proven out. When it happens, `.git` doesn't need manual copying (cloning recreates it), `node_modules`/caches/credentials never move, and curated `.obsidian`-style config (the stuff that took real configuration effort) does move. Here's Build 1's prompt for easy copy:

```
You are executing Build 1 of a cross-laptop sync project for an Obsidian vault. Read this entire prompt before acting. It is self-contained; you do not need any other context.

## Situation
Two Windows 11 laptops (a Dell, hostname DESKTOP-3VBG0JH, and a new Acer) need to share one Obsidian vault, "Jarvis", in near-real-time. The vault is at D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis and is a PUBLIC GitHub repo (gupta-builds/Jarvis) - nothing secret may ever leave this machine through any channel, including this build.

The chosen mechanism is Syncthing, confirmed correct after a dedicated pre-mortem (documented in 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap.md, read it now for full context before doing anything else). Two facts from that pre-mortem are non-negotiable constraints on this build:
1. Syncthing must NEVER sync the .git directory. Concurrent writes from two machines corrupt git's internal object store; this is documented, reproducible, and has destroyed real repositories for other users. This vault has its own .git folder since it is itself a git repo.
2. Obsidian's behavior when a note is open and being edited while an external process (Syncthing) writes a change to that same file is UNTESTED. You must test it, not assume an answer.

You are working on the Dell only in this build. Do not attempt to pair with the Acer, do not ask the user for the Acer's Syncthing Device ID, and do not install anything on any machine other than this one.

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap
## One-Line Answer
**Syncthing** carries the live **Jarvis** and **The Plan** vaults between the Dell and the Acer in near-real-time, **Git** keeps Jarvis's version history and GitHub backup on its own schedule, **Google Drive** (Mirror on the Dell, Stream on the Acer) carries plain document folders and the Desktop, and the existing same-machine **Unison** mirror gets two new entries for the Acer's Claude config. Codebases stay GitHub-only, deliberately excluded from Syncthing. Every build below also resolves 2-3 items from the vault's existing Obsidian plugin gap tracker, so that work rides along instead of becoming its own separate project.
## Why small builds instead of one setup
This has to run correctly once and never need repair. Build 0 (below) was a genuine pre-mortem, not a formality, run live in conversation with real research against Syncthing's own docs and forum. It surfaced one real corruption risk (Syncthing + `.git` in the same tree), confirmed the design is otherwise sound, and left one real unresolved question (Obsidian's behavior when a file is open and edited while Syncthing writes an incoming change) that needs a live test, not documentation. Each build from here runs in its own fresh Claude Code session with a self-contained prompt, so no build inherits an assumption nobody checked.
## Decisions locked in (2026-09-18)
Do not re-litigate these in a later build session:
- **Secrets** (`.claude`, `.cursor`, `.codex`, credentials, SSH keys) stay fresh-install-only per machine. Never copied, never synced, by any mechanism. Jarvis is a **public** GitHub repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`), which is part of why this matters.
- **Syncthing scope**: the Jarvis and The Plan vaults only. Confirmed, not reconsidered: syncing a live git working tree via Syncthing is a documented corruption path (Syncthing's own maintainer, and multiple forum reports of destroyed repos) because concurrent edits to `.git/` internals don't produce a consistent merge. Codebases stay GitHub push/pull only.
- **`.git/` exclusion**: every Syncthing folder's `.stignore` excludes `.git` completely, non-negotiable, including inside the Jarvis vault itself (Jarvis is also a git repo).
- **Google Drive**: consolidating from two accounts to one. Dell keeps Drive's Mirror mode (full local copy, already has the storage history). Acer uses Stream mode (cloud-first, cached on demand) to avoid the same cache/storage bloat that ruled out using Drive for the vaults. Desktop folder sync rides the same mechanism. This is separate from Syncthing on purpose: plain document folders don't need real-time bidirectional sync, just availability on both machines.
- **Discovery mode**: global discovery + relays. Verified: relay operators see device IDs, IPs, and traffic volume, never file names or content (content is end-to-end encrypted through the relay).
- **Git commit cadence for Jarvis**: automatic, scheduled.
- **`new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl`**: two new entries in the existing Unison `sync-manifest.json`, same shape as the other 9 project entries.
- **Browser sync (Vivaldi)**: out of scope.
- **Obsidian plugin documentation**: use the vault's existing system, don't invent a new one. Entry points are [[00 Plugin Reference Index]] (thematic map, one note per workflow, not per plugin) and [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]] (the live gap tracker with a "Needs Verification" list). Enrich existing thematic notes and resolve tracker items; only add a new thematic note if a workflow area genuinely has none.
## Build order
### Build 0 - Pre-mortem on the design (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
Run directly in conversation rather than delegated. Verdict: Syncthing remains the right tool (open-source, no server to maintain, matches "never touch it again" better than Resilio's closed-source client or Nextcloud's self-hosted server). `.git` exclusion confirmed mandatory. Obsidian file-lock behavior during a live Syncthing write is genuinely untested and becomes Build 1's first real test. Versioning (Staggered) recommended as the undo safety net, since Syncthing has no other rollback beyond it. A concrete rollback procedure is a precondition for Build 1, not an afterthought.
### Build 1 - Syncthing, Dell-only pilot + first plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Syncthing installed on the Dell (manual, user-side).
*Done when:* the Jarvis vault runs as a single Syncthing folder on the Dell alone, `.stignore` is verified to exclude `.git` and every secret path, Staggered versioning is on, a tested rollback procedure exists, and the Obsidian-file-lock scenario has been tested (even single-machine, by editing a file externally while it's open in Obsidian).
*Plugin-doc slice:* Local REST API security decision (port 27123 insecure server), Excalidraw visual-template gap, and the open "why do UI-visible community plugins exceed direct entries in `community-plugins.json`" question, all already named in [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]].
*Prompt:* [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 1 Prompt]].
### Build 2 - Acer pairing, first real two-machine sync + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 1 verified clean, Syncthing installed on the Acer.
*Done when:* the two laptops exchange Device IDs, the Jarvis folder syncs one real edit end to end, and a deliberate simultaneous-edit test on both machines produces the expected conflict-copy behavior, not data loss. This is the actual "live on both laptops" test.
*Plugin-doc slice:* QuickAdd capture-choices gap (empty `choices`, already flagged), Recent Files vs file-explorer-plus overlap, Tasks dashboard convention decision.
### Build 3 - Scheduled Git auto-commit/push + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* nothing from Builds 1-2, can run in parallel. Touches Jarvis's git history and a new Scheduled Task.
*Done when:* a script commits and pushes Jarvis on a schedule, survives a forced push-conflict with rebase-and-retry, and a manual dry run shows a clean exit with nothing staged when there's nothing to commit.
*Plugin-doc slice:* the Obsidian Git plugin's own auto-push/auto-pull cadence decision, already flagged in the gap tracker and directly relevant to this build's own work.
### Build 4 - Google Drive consolidation + Desktop sync + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* which Google account survives being named (open item below). Establishes the reusable pattern for future non-vault folders (class materials, etc.), per the user's "the base for it is laid out" requirement.
*Done when:* one Drive account carries the main document folders (Mirror on Dell, Stream on Acer) and the Desktop, verified with a real file added on one machine and checked on the other.
*Plugin-doc slice:* Omnisearch/Text Extractor decision (PDF/image indexing), since this build is also about which files are discoverable across machines.
### Build 5 - `new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl` wiring + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 2 (the vault needs to already be shared, since the Unison mirror folders these entries write into live inside it). `second-brain-claudekit` cloned on the Acer.
*Done when:* both manifest entries run clean on the Acer at least once and flip from `candidate` to `live`.
*Plugin-doc slice:* Copilot autonomous-tools decision (AI Automation and Local Interfaces), thematically matched to "new machine's AI config."
### Build 6 - Wrap-up note + final plugin-doc consolidation
*Depends on:* Builds 1-5 complete.
*Done when:* one summary note exists (in this same folder) recording every build's outcome, the crucial steps actually taken, and what's still open, written from this session's memory of each build rather than re-derived. The plugin gap tracker's "Needs Verification" list is revisited and updated to reflect what got resolved across Builds 1-5, not left stale.
## Explicitly deferred, not part of this numbered sequence
- **The Plan vault** gets the same treatment after Jarvis is fully proven out, reusing this build's pattern, faster. Not started.
- **Codebases** need their own sync strategy, deliberately not Syncthing (see the corruption risk above). Not started, acknowledged as the harder problem.
- **C: and D: drive cleanup** on both laptops. Explicitly noted as happening later, not part of active builds.
## Open items
- Which Google account survives the consolidation to one Drive sync, needed before Build 4 starts.
- Whether Build 1's local file-lock test is sufficient or needs a genuine second device sooner than Build 2.

You are executing Build 2 of a cross-laptop sync project for an Obsidian vault. Read this entire prompt before acting — it is self-contained and summarizes everything Builds 0-1 already established, so you do not need to re-derive it.

Read first, for full background: 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap.md (the strategy spine), 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 1 Findings.md (the file-lock test result), and 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Rollback Procedure.md (safety net if anything goes wrong).

Situation

Two Windows 11 laptops — a Dell (DESKTOP-3VBG0JH) and a new Acer — need to share the "Jarvis" Obsidian vault (D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis, a public GitHub repo gupta-builds/Jarvis) in near-real-time via Syncthing. Build 0 pre-mortemed the design; Build 1 (just completed) piloted it Dell-only. This build is the first real two-device test.

What Build 1 Actually Did (verbatim outcome, 2026-09-18)

- Syncthing was not pre-installed as assumed. It was installed manually mid-session as a portable v2.1.5 extract (not an installer, not a Windows service) at D:\Users\_Anant\Downloads\[REDACTED].1.5\. Config lives at C:\Users\Anant Gupta\AppData\Local\Syncthing\config.xml. This means it does not auto-start on reboot — if the Dell has been restarted since, check Get-Process syncthing before assuming it's running; the user may need to double-click syncthing.exe again, or you may need to set up autostart (that's arguably Build 3's Scheduled Task territory, not this build's).
- The Jarvis vault is added as a Send-Receive folder (id jarvis), Staggered versioning on with maxAge: 0 (indefinite retention), global discovery + relaying at their defaults (both enabled).
- .stignore exists at the vault root, matches the roadmap's required list exactly (no additions). Verified via Syncthing's own /rest/db/browse?prefix=.git that .git is fully unindexed — 4,019 files on disk, 0 seen by Syncthing.
- Final local scan: 106,498 files, 4.1GB, 0 errors, fully in sync (single device, nothing to pull yet).
- The file-lock test was run for real, not simulated on paper, using the jarvis MCP bridge to drive a live Obsidian pane plus a shell script to fake an external write. Two findings that matter for this build:
  a. Obsidian does not hold edits in a long-lived unsaved buffer — it flushes to disk within seconds regardless of Ctrl+S. So "is a file open and being edited" is a narrower window than you'd think.
  b. An external write silently replaces the live editor content instantly, with zero warning and no conflict-copy file. Staggered Versioning did not capture the discarded content either — because on a single device, nothing triggered Syncthing's own remote-driven replace/delete path, which is what its docs say actually creates a version. This is the open question Build 2 exists to close: does a genuine two-device sync produce a real .sync-conflict-* file and a real .stversions entry, or does it silently clob
- A rollback procedure exists and is sourced from Syncthing's own docs (pause-first via PATCH
  /rest/config/folders/jarvis {"pausl order, versioning recovery via.stversions/).
- .stignore + the findings note + fommitted to a new branchinfra/cross-laptop-sync (1 commit, 78b1ff67). Not merged to master, not pushed. Everything else in
  the working tree was left exactly ready was — that's pre-existing vault state, not something this build touched.
- Three plugin-gap tracker items gote along per the roadmap's design, not this build's main point): Local REST API's insecure port 27123 (it's a TLS gap, not an auth gap —

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap
## One-Line Answer
**Syncthing** carries the live **Jarvis** and **The Plan** vaults between the Dell and the Acer in near-real-time, **Git** keeps Jarvis's version history and GitHub backup on its own schedule, **Google Drive** (Mirror on the Dell, Stream on the Acer) carries plain document folders and the Desktop, and the existing same-machine **Unison** mirror gets two new entries for the Acer's Claude config. Codebases stay GitHub-only, deliberately excluded from Syncthing. Every build below also resolves 2-3 items from the vault's existing Obsidian plugin gap tracker, so that work rides along instead of becoming its own separate project. **Six builds remain** (2-7) before Jarvis is fully done; The Plan then reuses the proven pattern in roughly two prompts.
## Why small builds instead of one setup
This has to run correctly once and never need repair. Build 0 was a genuine pre-mortem, run live in conversation with real research against Syncthing's own docs and forum, and it correctly flagged that Obsidian's file-lock behavior during a live sync write was untested. Build 1 tested it for real (single machine) and found something the pre-mortem didn't fully anticipate: Staggered Versioning's own documentation ties it to a remote-device-driven replace, which a single-device test structurally cannot exercise. That gap is exactly why Build 2 exists before Build 3 (Acer pairing) rather than going straight to a second device: the safety net has to be proven, not assumed, before real vault data crosses a second machine. Each build runs in its own fresh Claude Code session with a self-contained prompt.
## Decisions locked in (2026-09-18)
Do not re-litigate these in a later build session:
- **Secrets** (`.claude`, `.cursor`, `.codex`, credentials, SSH keys) stay fresh-install-only per machine. Never copied, never synced, by any mechanism. Jarvis is a **public** GitHub repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`), which is part of why this matters.
- **Syncthing scope**: the Jarvis and The Plan vaults only. Confirmed, not reconsidered: syncing a live git working tree via Syncthing is a documented corruption path because concurrent edits to `.git/` internals don't produce a consistent merge. Codebases stay GitHub push/pull only.
- **`.git/` exclusion**: every Syncthing folder's `.stignore` excludes `.git` completely, non-negotiable, including inside the Jarvis vault itself.
- **Google Drive**: consolidating from two accounts to one. Dell keeps Mirror mode (full local copy). Acer uses Stream mode (cloud-first, cached on demand). Desktop folder sync rides the same mechanism. Separate from Syncthing on purpose: plain document folders don't need real-time bidirectional sync.
- **Discovery mode**: global discovery + relays. Verified: relay operators see device IDs, IPs, and traffic volume, never file names or content.
- **Git commit cadence for Jarvis**: automatic, scheduled.
- **`new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl`**: two new entries in the existing Unison `sync-manifest.json`, same shape as the other 9 project entries.
- **Browser sync (Vivaldi)**: out of scope.
- **Obsidian plugin documentation**: use the vault's existing system. Entry points are [[00 Plugin Reference Index]] and [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]]. Enrich existing thematic notes and resolve tracker items; only add a new thematic note if a workflow area genuinely has none.
- **No second device until the safety net is proven.** Build 1's single-machine test could not confirm Syncthing's conflict-copy or versioning actually engages for a real cross-device change. Build 2 proves this with two local Syncthing instances on the Dell before Build 3 ever installs on the Acer.
## Build order
### Build 0 - Pre-mortem on the design (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
Verdict: Syncthing remains the right tool. `.git` exclusion confirmed mandatory. Obsidian file-lock behavior flagged as genuinely untested, becoming Build 1's test. Staggered versioning recommended as the undo safety net.
### Build 1 - Syncthing, Dell-only pilot + first plugin-doc pass (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
*Outcome:* Syncthing installed manually as a portable v2.1.5 extract (not a Windows service, does not survive reboot yet — Build 2 fixes this). `.stignore` matches the roadmap's list exactly; `.git` confirmed fully unindexed via Syncthing's own REST API (4,019 files on disk, 0 seen by Syncthing). Final scan: 106,498 files, 4.1GB, 0 errors. Staggered versioning on, `maxAge: 0` (indefinite). The file-lock test found Obsidian flushes edits to disk almost immediately and an external write silently replaces live editor content with zero warning and no conflict file — but critically, **no `.stversions` entry was created either**, and per Syncthing's own versioning docs this is expected on a single device: versioning triggers on a remote-driven replace, which this test structurally couldn't produce. This is the open question Build 2 exists to close, not a settled result. All three plugin items resolved. Full detail: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 1 Findings]], [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Rollback Procedure]]. Committed to `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (commit `78b1ff67`), not merged, not pushed.
### Build 2 - Prove the safety net, harden Syncthing, no second device yet
*Depends on:* Build 1 complete (done).
*Done when:* two independent local Syncthing instances (distinct `-home`, distinct ports, paired over `127.0.0.1`, no internet/relay needed) sync a **disposable test folder** — never the real vault — and a genuine cross-instance edit produces a real `.sync-conflict-*` file, and a genuine cross-instance replace lands a version in `.stversions/`. This is the exact mechanism Build 3 will rely on with the Acer, proven safe first without any real data or a second physical machine at risk. The second instance and test folder are torn down cleanly afterward, no stray running process or leftover config. Separately, Syncthing is made to survive a reboot/logon reliably (currently a manually-launched portable exe).
*Plugin-doc slice:* QuickAdd capture-choices gap, Recent Files vs file-explorer-plus overlap, Tasks dashboard convention decision.
*Prompt:* [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 2 Prompt]].
### Build 3 - Acer pairing, first real two-machine sync + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 2 verified clean, Syncthing installed on the Acer (manual, user-side — do not install it for them).
*Done when:* the two laptops exchange Device IDs, the Jarvis folder syncs one real edit end to end, and a deliberate simultaneous-edit test on both machines reproduces what Build 2 already proved locally, this time for real.
*Plugin-doc slice:* whether Calendar is installed or only referenced by old hotkeys, whether Lazy Plugin Loader's reference to `excalibrain` (no matching plugin folder found) is a real gap, whether Workspaces Plus was intentionally removed.
### Build 4 - Scheduled Git auto-commit/push + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* nothing from Builds 2-3, can run in parallel. Touches Jarvis's git history and a new Scheduled Task.
*Done when:* a script commits and pushes Jarvis on a schedule, survives a forced push-conflict with rebase-and-retry, and a manual dry run shows a clean exit with nothing staged when there's nothing to commit.
*Plugin-doc slice:* the Obsidian Git plugin's own auto-push/auto-pull cadence decision, already flagged in the gap tracker.
### Build 5 - Google Drive consolidation + Desktop sync + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* which Google account survives being named (open item below).
*Done when:* one Drive account carries the main document folders (Mirror on Dell, Stream on Acer) and the Desktop, verified with a real file added on one machine and checked on the other.
*Plugin-doc slice:* Omnisearch/Text Extractor decision (PDF/image indexing).
### Build 6 - `new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl` wiring + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 3. `second-brain-claudekit` cloned on the Acer.
*Done when:* both manifest entries run clean on the Acer at least once and flip from `candidate` to `live`.
*Plugin-doc slice:* Copilot autonomous-tools decision.
### Build 7 - Wrap-up note + final plugin-doc consolidation
*Depends on:* Builds 2-6 complete.
*Done when:* one summary note exists recording every build's outcome and what's still open. The plugin gap tracker's "Needs Verification" list is revisited and updated.
## Codebases: proposed strategy (documented, not yet executed)
Two things narrow this down. First, once Jarvis's Syncthing is live, the existing same-machine Unison mirrors (`.claude`/`CLAUDE.md` per project under `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/`) start propagating cross-laptop for free, since Unison writes into the vault Syncthing already carries — a real slice of "codebase-adjacent" state is solved without touching code directly. Second, unlike the vault, code isn't usually being typed into on both laptops at the same moment, so it doesn't need real-time sync, only discipline: commit and push before switching machines. The proposed approach is to generalize Build 4's auto-commit script to also run against a short, explicitly named list of actively-dual-laptop repos, not all ~58 repos under `~/projects`, and leave the rest to manual git hygiene. This does not need new sync infrastructure. Blocking, independent of any of this: `second-brain-claudekit` had 46 unpushed commits and `internship-research-loop` was diverged (ahead 5, behind 7) as of the 2026-09-16 session — needs a real cleanup pass before any dual-laptop code workflow means anything, tooling or not.
## Explicitly deferred, not part of this numbered sequence
- **The Plan vault** gets the same treatment after Jarvis is fully proven out. Goal: roughly two prompts, since Build 2's conflict/versioning proof and `.stignore` reasoning are being written generically enough to reuse rather than re-prove from scratch.
- **Codebases**: strategy proposed above, execution not started.
- **C: and D: drive cleanup** on both laptops. Explicitly later, not part of active builds.
## Open items
- Which Google account survives the consolidation to one Drive sync, needed before Build 5 starts.
