# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 2 Prompt
Paste the block below into a fresh Claude Code session opened in this vault (`D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`). Self-contained. Full context: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap]].
```
You are executing Build 2 of a cross-laptop sync project for an Obsidian vault. Read this entire prompt before acting. Then read, in this order: 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap.md (full strategy and where this build sits), 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 1 Findings.md (why this build exists), and 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Rollback Procedure.md (how to back out if anything looks wrong).

## Why this build exists
The vault ("Jarvis", D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis, a PUBLIC GitHub repo gupta-builds/Jarvis) needs to sync in near-real-time between two laptops via Syncthing. Build 1 piloted Syncthing on this machine (the Dell) alone and found something important: when a file changed externally, Obsidian silently reloaded it with zero warning, no conflict file, and no version saved to .stversions. Syncthing's own documentation says versioning triggers when a file is "replaced or deleted on a remote device" - a single-device test cannot exercise that path at all. So the honest state right now is: we do not know if Syncthing's conflict-copy and versioning safety nets actually work. The user has explicitly decided NOT to install Syncthing on the second laptop (an Acer) until this is proven, because doing so with real vault data at risk before knowing the answer is exactly the mistake this whole staged approach exists to avoid. Do not install anything on, or attempt to reach, any machine other than this one.

## Your job, in order

### 1. Prove the safety net using two local Syncthing instances, not a second physical device
Run a second, independent Syncthing instance on this same Dell, with its own identity, so you can test genuine cross-device sync behavior safely:
- Start it with a distinct -home directory (its own config.xml, its own device ID) and distinct GUI/listen ports so it does not collide with the already-running primary instance (currently PIDs visible via `Get-Process syncthing`, portable install at D:\Users\_Anant\Downloads\[REDACTED].1.5\, primary config at %LOCALAPPDATA%\Syncthing\config.xml - verify this is still accurate, do not assume it hasn't changed).
- Research Syncthing's own documentation for running multiple instances on one machine and for pairing two instances over 127.0.0.1 without needing internet discovery or relaying (this should work purely locally). Cite what you find.
- Add the two instances as devices to each other and share ONE folder between them, pointed at a DISPOSABLE test directory on each side - something like C:\SyncTest\InstanceA and C:\SyncTest\InstanceB. Do NOT point this test folder at the real Jarvis vault, and do NOT reuse the real vault's Syncthing folder ID for this test. Put a handful of small representative markdown files in it, nothing sensitive.
- Run two specific experiments and record exactly what happens for each:
  a. Edit the same test file independently on both instances within Syncthing's detection window (i.e. before they've had a chance to sync the first change). Confirm whether a real .sync-conflict-<date>-<time>-<deviceID>.ext file appears, matching Syncthing's documented behavior.
  b. Reproduce Build 1's exact race, but for real this time: begin editing a file on Instance A (simulating an in-progress edit on one laptop), then immediately let Instance B push a conflicting change into the shared folder (simulating an incoming sync from the other laptop) before Instance A's edit would normally settle. Check whether Instance A's discarded content lands in .stversions/ this time, since this is a genuine remote-driven replace, unlike Build 1's local-only test.
- When both experiments are done, tear down the second instance cleanly: stop the process, remove its config directory, delete the disposable test folders on both sides. Leave no second Syncthing instance running, no leftover scheduled task, no stray folder config. Verify the primary instance and the real Jarvis vault folder are completely untouched by this entire step (check the primary instance's own folder list and the real vault's file count/hash state before and after).

### 2. Make Syncthing survive a reboot
It currently only runs because someone manually launched the exe; it will not come back after a restart or logout. Research the correct, currently-recommended way to make Syncthing start automatically on this machine (a Scheduled Task at logon is one common approach; Syncthing's own documentation may recommend something more specific for a portable Windows install versus an installed one - check before choosing). Implement it, then verify it actually works (log off/on if you can safely do so, or find another way to confirm the trigger is correctly registered without requiring a full reboot if that is disruptive to check firsthand - use judgment, but do not just claim it works without some form of verification). This is scoped to Syncthing's own persistence only; do NOT build the Jarvis git auto-commit Scheduled Task here, that is a later, separate build.

### 3. Plugin documentation slice
Using 40_Resources/Obsidian/Plugins/Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification.md as the tracker and 40_Resources/Obsidian/Plugins/00 Plugin Reference Index.md as the map, research and document (do not change actual plugin settings) these three items, each with an official source citation, following this vault's existing conventions (read HUMAN_WRITING.md first, preserve frontmatter, no blank lines except after a callout):
   a. QuickAdd's empty choices configuration gap - document what a sensible first set of choices would look like and how to configure them, in 40_Resources/Obsidian/Plugins/QuickAdd Capture Menu.md.
   b. Whether Recent Files and file-explorer-plus overlap in function or serve genuinely different purposes, in 40_Resources/Obsidian/Plugins/Search Linking and Navigation.md.
   c. The Tasks-plugin-vs-Dataview-TASK-block convention question already named in the gap tracker (00_Dashboard uses a Dataview TASK block; is that still the right call), in 40_Resources/Obsidian/Plugins/Tasks Kanban and Project Tracking.md.
   Update the gap tracker note itself to reflect what got resolved.

## Research standard
Every claim about Syncthing's multi-instance setup, conflict behavior, versioning behavior, and autostart method must be verified this session against Syncthing's own current documentation or its live REST API on this machine, not general knowledge or Build 1's findings assumed to generalize. Cite sources.

## Boundaries
Do not install Syncthing on, or attempt to reach, the Acer or any machine besides this one. Do not touch the real Jarvis vault's Syncthing folder configuration except to verify it is undisturbed. Do not create the Jarvis git auto-commit Scheduled Task (a later build). Do not edit sync-manifest.json or touch Google Drive settings (later builds). Do not change any Obsidian plugin's actual settings, only document tradeoffs. If you hit a genuine ambiguity this prompt doesn't resolve, ask the user rather than guessing - this build is explicitly about not assuming things work.

## When finished
Write results to a new note, 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 2 Findings.md, matching the style of Build 1's findings note (One-Line Answer, Test Setup, What Actually Happened, Sources). Then update the Build 2 entry in 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap.md to mark it complete with a short factual outcome, matching how the Build 1 entry now reads. Continue committing on the infra/cross-laptop-sync branch (do not merge to master, do not push). This is the only file besides the new findings note to edit; do not rewrite other sections of the roadmap.

```
---
name: feedback-prompt-notes-no-chat-duplicate
description: When a handoff prompt gets written into a vault note as a fenced code block, don't also paste the full prompt text into the chat reply - the note's own code fence already gives a one-click copy button in Obsidian
metadata:
  type: feedback
---

Rule: when a deliverable is a prompt (or any large copyable block) written into an Obsidian vault note inside a ```...``` fence, do not additionally repeat that full text in the chat response. Obsidian renders a copy button on fenced code blocks natively, so the note itself is already the easy-copy source - restating it in chat is pure duplication.

Why: user said directly, "You just spent a lot of tokens printing the same thing in both places, by easier copying i meant for it to take place inside obsidian itself. That's was easier copy: ```...``` makes a copy button for the entire text inside it - easy." Confirmed 2026-09-19 during [[project_jarvis_cross_laptop_sync]] Build 1/2 handoff-prompt work.

How to apply: after writing a fenced prompt/block into a vault note, the chat reply should point at the note and summarize what changed, not reproduce the fenced content a second time. ## Claude

Let me verify Build 2's claims directly rather than take the report at face value, and check the actual current state of everything before deciding what's next.

All of Build 2's claims check out against the live system, with one thing worth flagging: my first Scheduled Task check came back empty (false negative from a stale PowerShell cache), a second broader query confirmed the `Syncthing` task really does exist, action `serve --no-console --no-browser` against the correct exe, execution time limit disabled — exactly as reported. `.stignore`, the git commit, the plugin notes, and the clean teardown (`C:\SyncTest` gone, primary instance's two original PIDs unchanged) all match what was claimed. Good, trustworthy report.

## Direct answers

**Are we ready to turn Syncthing on for real?** Not quite. The mechanism itself is proven correct now (conflict-copy and versioning genuinely work cross-device), but there's one residual gap that's procedural, not fixable in config: an edit made in the few seconds before Syncthing's file watcher notices it can still get silently overwritten with no safety net, on either machine. That's a "don't edit the same laptop-adjacent note on both machines in the same few seconds" discipline issue, not a blocker to pairing. What *is* still missing before pairing: a way to verify both machines actually hold identical content (see below), and I agree with you that a settings audit should happen first, since nobody has yet gone looking for what else in `.obsidian` might cause sync churn or conflicts.

**GitHub/branch setup for two laptops.** I checked the actual history: this repo has always committed straight to `master`, no PR workflow, no branch protection — normal for a single-owner personal vault. I don't think that needs to change. The reason a multi-branch model exists for team repos (avoiding two people's concurrent work colliding) doesn't apply the same way here, because Syncthing is what keeps both laptops' *working trees* converged in near real time — by the time either machine's scheduled git commit fires, both copies are almost always already identical, so a direct commit to `master` has nothing to diverge from. The actual risk isn't git branching, it's two scheduled auto-commit tasks (once built) racing to push at the same moment — already planned to handle that with pull-rebase-retry, no new branch model needed. I'm writing this conclusion into the roadmap rather than leaving it open.

**Unison mirror folders and Syncthing.** Verified directly against the real `.stignore`: it excludes only the dot-prefixed raw home-dir dumps (`.claude_windows`, `.claude_wsl`, `.cursor_windows`, `.cursor_wsl`, `.kiro_windows`, `.kiro_wsl`) and the raw AI-conversation transcript folders. The regular per-project mirror folders Unison actually writes into — `20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/second-brain-claudekit/`, `CausalOps/`, and eventually `new-laptop-windows/`, `new-laptop-wsl/` — are **not** excluded, so yes, they sync through Syncthing like any other vault content. No symlinks are involved anywhere in this; Unison writes real files, and Syncthing syncs real files. Worth double-checking directly rather than trusting my memory of the design, so I've put that verification in the next build too.

**What's excluded, what's guaranteed to sync.** Excluded: everything in `.stignore` (secrets, `.git`, the raw dumps above, generated sqlite caches, a handful of secret-bearing plugin `data.json` files, Obsidian's workspace/cache state). Everything else in the vault syncs, including most `.obsidian` settings.

**How do we know both laptops actually hold the same file?** Don't build a separate hashing tool — Syncthing already computes and exposes this. `GET /rest/db/completion?folder=jarvis&device=<id>` reports `completion: 100` and `needBytes: 0` only when a device is genuinely fully synced, and `/rest/db/status` shows folder state (`idle` vs `syncing`/`outOfSync`). I'm having the next build turn this into an actual repeatable verification step instead of leaving it as a fact I just stated.

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap
## One-Line Answer
**Syncthing** carries the live **Jarvis** and **The Plan** vaults between the Dell and the Acer in near-real-time, **Git** keeps Jarvis's version history and GitHub backup on its own schedule (direct-to-`master`, no branch-per-machine — see Decisions below), **Google Drive** (Mirror on the Dell, Stream on the Acer) carries plain document folders and the Desktop, and the existing same-machine **Unison** mirror gets two new entries for the Acer's Claude config. Codebases stay GitHub-only, deliberately excluded from Syncthing. Every build below also resolves several items from the vault's existing Obsidian plugin gap tracker. **Seven builds remain** (3-9) before Jarvis is fully done; The Plan then reuses the proven pattern in roughly two prompts.
## Why small builds instead of one setup
This has to run correctly once and never need repair. Build 0's pre-mortem flagged Obsidian's file-lock behavior as untested; Build 1 tested it single-machine and found a real gap (no conflict file, no version, on a plain external overwrite); Build 2 proved the mechanism cross-device using two local Syncthing instances and found the gap survives, narrowed to a specific short window (an edit Syncthing's watcher hasn't scanned yet). Each build runs in its own fresh Claude Code session with a self-contained prompt, so no build inherits an assumption nobody checked.
## Decisions locked in
Do not re-litigate these in a later build session:
- **Secrets** (`.claude`, `.cursor`, `.codex`, credentials, SSH keys) stay fresh-install-only per machine. Never copied, never synced, by any mechanism. Jarvis is a **public** GitHub repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`).
- **Syncthing scope**: the Jarvis and The Plan vaults only. Codebases stay GitHub push/pull only — syncing a live git working tree via Syncthing is a documented corruption path.
- **`.git/` exclusion**: every Syncthing folder's `.stignore` excludes `.git` completely, non-negotiable.
- **Google Drive**: consolidating from two accounts to one. Dell Mirror mode, Acer Stream mode. Desktop folder sync rides the same mechanism, separate from Syncthing on purpose.
- **Discovery mode**: global discovery + relays. Verified: relay operators see device IDs, IPs, and traffic volume, never file names or content.
- **Git commit cadence for Jarvis**: automatic, scheduled.
- **`new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl`**: two new entries in the existing Unison `sync-manifest.json`, same shape as the other 9 project entries. Verified 2026-09-19: these mirror folders are not excluded by `.stignore`, so they will propagate cross-laptop via Syncthing once created — no symlinks involved anywhere in the Unison pipeline, it writes and reads real files.
- **Browser sync (Vivaldi)**: out of scope.
- **Obsidian plugin documentation**: use the vault's existing system. Entry points are [[00 Plugin Reference Index]] and [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]]. Six items resolved so far across Builds 1-2 (see their outcomes below) — do not redo these.
- **No second device until the safety net is proven.** Confirmed by Build 2. The residual risk (an edit in the short pre-scan window can still be silently overwritten) is procedural, not fixable in config — avoid editing the same note on both laptops within the same few seconds, don't treat it as a blocker.
- **Git branch strategy for two laptops: stays direct-to-`master`, no per-machine branching.** Reconsidered 2026-09-19 at the user's request. This repo has always committed straight to `master` (no PR workflow, single owner) and that doesn't need to change: Syncthing, not git, is what keeps both laptops' working trees converged in near real time, so by the time either machine's scheduled git commit fires, both copies are almost always already identical and there's nothing to diverge from. The actual risk is two scheduled auto-commit tasks racing to push at the same moment, which Build 5's pull-rebase-retry design already covers — a branch model wouldn't fix that anyway.
- **Cross-machine sync verification**: use Syncthing's own REST API (`/rest/db/completion?folder=jarvis&device=<id>` for `completion: 100`/`needBytes: 0`, `/rest/db/status` for folder state) rather than inventing separate hashing/diffing tooling. Build 3 turns this into an actual repeatable check.
## Build order
### Build 0 - Pre-mortem on the design (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
Verdict: Syncthing remains the right tool. `.git` exclusion mandatory. Obsidian file-lock behavior flagged as untested.
### Build 1 - Syncthing, Dell-only pilot + first plugin-doc pass (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
*Outcome:* Syncthing installed as a portable v2.1.5 extract. `.stignore` created and verified (`.git` fully unindexed). Staggered versioning on. File-lock test found a silent-overwrite gap on a single machine. Resolved: Local REST API security tradeoff ([[AI Automation and Local Interfaces]]), Excalidraw template how-to ([[Excalidraw Diagrams and Annotation]]), Lazy Plugin Loader mechanism for why the UI shows more plugins than `community-plugins.json` lists ([[Plugin Inventory and Configuration Map]]). Full detail: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 1 Findings]]. Committed `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (`78b1ff67`).
### Build 2 - Prove the safety net, harden Syncthing, no second device yet (COMPLETE, 2026-09-19)
*Outcome:* Two independent local Syncthing instances confirmed real `.sync-conflict-*` files and `.stversions/` entries on genuine cross-device replaces — the mechanism works. Also confirmed the silent-loss gap survives cross-device: an edit the watcher hasn't scanned yet still gets overwritten with nothing saved. Syncthing now autostarts via a logon-triggered Scheduled Task (verified registered and correctly configured 2026-09-19; a real cold-boot cycle still hasn't run). Resolved: QuickAdd capture-choices how-to ([[QuickAdd Capture Menu]]), Recent Files vs file-explorer-plus — no overlap ([[Search Linking and Navigation]]), Tasks-vs-Dataview dashboard convention — stays Dataview ([[Tasks Kanban and Project Tracking]]). Full detail: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 2 Findings]]. Committed `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (`f680af4c`).
### Build 3 - Settings/conflict audit, verification tooling, heavy plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 2 complete (done). No second device involved.
*Done when:* every `.obsidian` settings file has been checked for machine-specific absolute paths or high-churn state that would misbehave once a second laptop is in the loop, `.stignore` is confirmed still correct (additions justified, not assumed) or amended with reasoning, a working cross-machine sync-verification check exists using Syncthing's own REST API, and the Unison-mirror-folders-sync-through-Syncthing claim above is independently re-verified rather than trusted from memory.
*Plugin-doc slice:* at least 3 of the ~10 remaining tracker items, heavier than prior builds — see the prompt for the full list and which 6 are already done.
*Prompt:* [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 3 Prompt]].
### Build 4 - Acer pairing, first real two-machine sync + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 3 verified clean, Syncthing installed on the Acer (manual, user-side).
*Done when:* the two laptops exchange Device IDs, the Jarvis folder syncs one real edit end to end, a deliberate simultaneous-edit test reproduces what Build 2 already proved locally, and Build 3's verification check confirms both machines report full completion.
*Plugin-doc slice:* whether Calendar is installed or only referenced by old hotkeys, whether Lazy Plugin Loader's reference to `excalibrain` is a real gap, whether Workspaces Plus was intentionally removed (unless Build 3 already claimed these).
### Build 5 - Scheduled Git auto-commit/push + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* nothing from Builds 3-4, can run in parallel. Touches Jarvis's git history and a new Scheduled Task.
*Done when:* a script commits and pushes Jarvis on a schedule, survives a forced push-conflict with rebase-and-retry, and a manual dry run shows a clean exit with nothing staged when there's nothing to commit.
*Plugin-doc slice:* the Obsidian Git plugin's own auto-push/auto-pull cadence decision.
### Build 6 - Google Drive consolidation + Desktop sync + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* which Google account survives being named (open item below).
*Done when:* one Drive account carries the main document folders (Mirror on Dell, Stream on Acer) and the Desktop, verified with a real file added on one machine and checked on the other.
*Plugin-doc slice:* Omnisearch/Text Extractor decision.
### Build 7 - `new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl` wiring + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 4. `second-brain-claudekit` cloned on the Acer.
*Done when:* both manifest entries run clean on the Acer at least once and flip from `candidate` to `live`.
*Plugin-doc slice:* Copilot autonomous-tools decision.
### Build 8 - Wrap-up note + final plugin-doc consolidation
*Depends on:* Builds 3-7 complete.
*Done when:* one summary note exists recording every build's outcome and what's still open. The plugin gap tracker's "Needs Verification" list is revisited and updated.
## Codebases: proposed strategy (documented, not yet executed)
Once Jarvis's Syncthing is live, the existing same-machine Unison mirrors start propagating cross-laptop for free, since Unison writes into the vault Syncthing already carries. For the code itself: no new sync infrastructure, just git discipline, since code isn't typed into on both laptops at once the way notes are. Generalize Build 5's auto-commit script to a short, explicitly named list of actively-dual-laptop repos, leave the rest to manual git hygiene. Blocking, independent of tooling: `second-brain-claudekit` had 46 unpushed commits and `internship-research-loop` was diverged as of 2026-09-16 — needs a real cleanup pass first.

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 3 Prompt
Paste the block below into a fresh Claude Code session opened in this vault (`D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`). Self-contained. Full context: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap]].

You are executing Build 3 of a cross-laptop sync project for an Obsidian vault. Read this entire prompt first. Then read, in order: 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap.md (full strategy, decisions already locked in, build order), 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 2 Findings.md (why the residual risk window matters), and the current D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\.stignore.

## Why this build exists
Builds 1-2 proved Syncthing's conflict-copy and versioning mechanism works correctly on a genuine two-device sync, with one known narrow gap (an edit made before Syncthing's watcher scans it can be silently overwritten). No second physical device (the Acer) has been touched yet, on purpose - the user wants Jarvis's own setup fully audited and verifiable before that happens. This build does NOT pair with the Acer and does NOT install anything on any other machine.

## Your job, in four parts

### 1. Settings and conflict audit
Go through this vault's actual .obsidian settings files (app.json, core-plugins.json, community-plugins.json, hotkeys.json, appearance.json, and any plugin data.json NOT already in .stignore) looking specifically for two failure classes that would matter once a second laptop is in the loop:
- Machine-specific absolute paths (a different username, drive letter, or install path on the Acer would make a synced setting point at something that doesn't exist there).
- State that churns constantly for reasons that have nothing to do with real content changes (this creates sync noise and raises the odds of hitting the exact race window Build 2 found).
For each finding, decide whether it changes what .stignore should exclude. Do not edit .stignore based on a guess - if you find something, state the specific setting, the specific risk, and your recommendation, and only change .stignore if the reasoning is solid enough to write down clearly. Read 40_Resources/Obsidian/Plugins/Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification.md first; several of these paths are already partially documented there (e.g. Obsidian Git's auto-push interval, dirty-worktree behavior) - don't rediscover what's already known, build on it.

### 2. Verify the Unison-to-Syncthing claim independently
The roadmap states that Unison's per-project mirror folders under 20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/ are real files (not symlinks) and are not excluded by .stignore, so they will sync via Syncthing. Confirm this yourself rather than trusting the roadmap's memory of it: check the actual file type of at least one mirrored file on disk (e.g. via a PowerShell Get-Item and checking LinkType, or an equivalent check), and confirm against the current .stignore that none of the named project folders (second-brain-claudekit, CausalOps, etc.) appear in it. Write a short confirmation (or correction, if you find the claim is wrong) into your findings note.

### 3. Build a real cross-machine sync-verification check
Using Syncthing's own REST API on this machine (GET /rest/db/completion?folder=jarvis&device=<id> and GET /rest/db/status?folder=jarvis against http://127.0.0.1:8384, using the API key from the primary instance's config.xml), write a small, documented procedure (a short script or a precise set of steps, your judgment on which is more useful here) that answers "are both laptops' Jarvis folders actually fully synced right now" without inventing separate hashing. This only needs to work against this single instance today, since there's no second device yet - but write it so Build 4 can point it at two real devices without rework. Document it as a new section in 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Rollback Procedure.md (it belongs next to the rollback material, not as a separate note) or a new note if you judge that cleaner - explain your choice either way.

### 4. Heavy plugin-documentation pass
Resolve at least 3, ideally more, of these still-open items from 40_Resources/Obsidian/Plugins/Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification.md's "Needs Verification" list. These 6 are ALREADY DONE across Builds 1-2, do not redo them: Local REST API security tradeoff, Excalidraw template how-to, Lazy Plugin Loader / community-plugins.json mechanism, QuickAdd capture-choices how-to, Recent Files vs file-explorer-plus overlap, Tasks-vs-Dataview dashboard convention. Pick from what remains, for example: the four lazy-loaded plugins not yet in the vault's plugin inventory (lean-terminal, homepage, obsidian-meta-bind-plugin, multi-column-markdown - research what each actually does and give each a home, either an existing thematic note or a new one if none fits, per this vault's existing convention of grouping by workflow not by plugin), Local REST API's port 27123 binding-host scope, whether Excalidraw auto-export should be enabled, Tasks date/priority conventions, Kanban lane names, whether Calendar/Publish/Bases/Workspaces Plus are actually in use or leftover references, Spaced Repetition review cadence. For each item you resolve: cite at least one official source, follow this vault's writing conventions (read HUMAN_WRITING.md first, preserve frontmatter, no blank lines except after a callout), and update the gap tracker note itself to reflect what got resolved, matching how Builds 1-2 did it.

## Research standard
Every claim (which .obsidian settings are risky, whether the Unison folders really aren't excluded, what the Syncthing REST API actually returns, what each researched plugin actually does) must be verified this session against the live system or current official documentation, not assumed from the roadmap or general knowledge.

## Boundaries
Do not install Syncthing on, or attempt to reach, the Acer or any other machine. Do not pair any new device. Do not create the Jarvis git auto-commit Scheduled Task (a later build). Do not edit sync-manifest.json or touch Google Drive settings (later builds). Do not change any Obsidian plugin's actual settings, only document tradeoffs, except .stignore itself if part 1's reasoning genuinely calls for a change. Continue on the infra/cross-laptop-sync branch, do not merge to master, do not push. If you hit a genuine ambiguity this prompt doesn't resolve, ask the user rather than guessing.

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap
## One-Line Answer
**Syncthing** carries the live **Jarvis** and **The Plan** vaults between the Dell and the Acer in near-real-time, **Git** keeps Jarvis's version history and GitHub backup on its own schedule (direct-to-`master`), **Google Drive** (Mirror on the Dell, Stream on the Acer) carries plain document folders and the Desktop, and the existing same-machine **Unison** mirror gets two new entries for the Acer's Claude config. `.stignore` excludes only genuine secrets and proven sync-conflict-causing churn, nothing else — curated config mirrors sync like everything else. Codebases stay GitHub-only. **Six builds remain** (4-9) before Jarvis is fully done; The Plan then reuses the proven pattern in roughly two prompts.
## Why small builds instead of one setup
This has to run correctly once and never need repair. Build 0's pre-mortem flagged Obsidian's file-lock behavior as untested; Build 1 found a real single-machine gap; Build 2 proved the mechanism cross-device and found the gap survives, narrowed to a short window; Build 3 audited settings, built real verification tooling, and caught two outright errors in the existing plugin tracker by checking the filesystem instead of trusting old notes. That same discipline (verify, don't assume) is why Build 4 exists before Acer pairing: a real content audit of six folders before broadening what syncs, not just taking a stated intention at face value.
## Decisions locked in
Do not re-litigate these in a later build session:
- **Secrets** (`.claude`, `.cursor`, `.codex`, credentials, SSH keys) stay fresh-install-only per machine. Never copied, never synced, by any mechanism. Jarvis is a **public** GitHub repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`).
- **Syncthing scope**: the Jarvis and The Plan vaults only. Codebases stay GitHub push/pull only.
- **`.git/` exclusion**: every Syncthing folder's `.stignore` excludes `.git` completely, non-negotiable.
- **`.stignore` philosophy, revised 2026-09-19**: exclude only genuine secrets and settings proven to cause sync churn/conflicts (like Build 3's `lean-terminal`/`recent-files-obsidian` findings), not broad categories excluded "to be safe." The six curated home-dir mirror folders (`.claude_windows`, `.claude_wsl`, `.cursor_windows`, `.cursor_wsl`, `.kiro_windows`, `.kiro_wsl`) were excluded in Build 1 as a conservative default without verifying their actual contents — a manual spot-check 2026-09-19 found only config files (`agents/`, `commands/`, `hooks/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `Setup.md`, `Sync-Log.md`, `skills/`), no credentials or session transcripts, but `.gitignore`'s own comment for these folders says "sessions, history, creds" — a real contradiction. Build 4's first task is a full automated secret-pattern scan as the actual gate before removing any of the six from `.stignore`, not the spot-check alone.
- **Google Drive**: consolidating from two accounts to one. Dell Mirror mode, Acer Stream mode.
- **Discovery mode**: global discovery + relays.
- **Git commit cadence for Jarvis**: automatic, scheduled.
- **`new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl`**: two new entries in the Unison `sync-manifest.json`. These mirror folders are real files, not symlinks, and sync via Syncthing once created.
- **Browser sync (Vivaldi)**: out of scope.
- **Obsidian plugin documentation**: use the vault's existing system ([[00 Plugin Reference Index]], [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]]). 15 items resolved across Builds 1-3 — see their outcomes below, do not redo.
- **No second device until the safety net is proven.** Confirmed by Build 2. Residual risk (pre-scan-window edits can be silently overwritten) is procedural, not fixable in config.
- **Git branch strategy for two laptops stays direct-to-`master`.** Syncthing keeps both working trees converged in near real time, so there's rarely anything to diverge; the real risk is two scheduled auto-commit tasks racing to push, handled by Build 6's rebase-and-retry design, not a branch model.
- **Cross-machine sync verification**: Syncthing's own REST API (`check-syncthing-status.ps1`, built in Build 3), not separate hashing tooling.
- **Claude Code hook friction**: a `PreToolUse:Edit` hook false-positive-blocks direct edits to `.stignore` (treats it as a new vault-root file). Known, not yet fixed — Build 4 and beyond use the same `sed`-through-Bash workaround Build 3 found. Fixing the hook itself is a Claude Code settings change outside this vault and needs the user's explicit go-ahead, not assumed.
## Build order
### Build 0 - Pre-mortem on the design (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
### Build 1 - Syncthing, Dell-only pilot + first plugin-doc pass (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
*Outcome:* Syncthing installed, `.stignore` created, Staggered versioning on. File-lock test found a silent-overwrite gap single-machine. Resolved 3 plugin items. Full detail: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 1 Findings]]. `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (`78b1ff67`).
### Build 2 - Prove the safety net, harden Syncthing, no second device yet (COMPLETE, 2026-09-19)
*Outcome:* Two local Syncthing instances confirmed real conflict-copy and versioning behavior cross-device; confirmed the silent-loss gap survives, narrowed to the pre-scan window. Autostart Scheduled Task registered and verified. Resolved 3 plugin items. Full detail: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 2 Findings]]. `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (`f680af4c`).
### Build 3 - Settings/conflict audit, verification tooling, heavy plugin-doc pass (COMPLETE, 2026-09-19)
*Outcome:* Audited every `.obsidian` setting and plugin `data.json`; added `lean-terminal` and `recent-files-obsidian` data files to `.stignore` (machine-specific paths / pure churn). Independently re-verified the Unison-mirror-folders-sync claim. Built and live-tested `check-syncthing-status.ps1` against Syncthing's REST API, documented in [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Rollback Procedure]]'s Scenario 4. Resolved or corrected 9 plugin items, catching two errors in the old tracker (`excalibrain` actually installed, `workspaces-plus` is leftover `.bak` files). Full detail: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 3 Findings]]. `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (further commit after `f680af4c`).
### Build 4 - Broaden `.stignore` to secrets-only, check for new race surfaces, log hygiene
*Depends on:* Build 3 complete (done). No second device involved.
*Done when:* a full automated secret-pattern scan of the six curated mirror folders is complete and documented; each of the six is removed from `.stignore` only if the scan comes back clean for it (if any folder fails, it stays excluded and the finding is reported, not worked around); the entire current `.stignore` is re-reviewed line by line against the secrets-and-proven-churn-only philosophy, with each remaining line justified in writing; whether Unison's 15-minute local writes into these now-synced folders create a race analogous to Build 1/2's finding is reasoned through (and live-checked with `check-syncthing-status.ps1` if practical); a log-rotation/cleanup policy is proposed for `Sync-Log.md`/`_All-Projects-Sync-Log.md` (implemented only if it stays within this vault's own scripts, not the `second-brain-claudekit` repo, without separate authorization); and Build 3's findings note is corrected/strengthened to reflect the new `.stignore` philosophy rather than left describing the old one.
*Prompt:* [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 4 Prompt]].
### Build 5 - Acer pairing, first real two-machine sync + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 4 verified clean, Syncthing installed on the Acer (manual, user-side).
*Done when:* the two laptops exchange Device IDs, the Jarvis folder syncs one real edit end to end, a deliberate simultaneous-edit test reproduces what Build 2 already proved locally, and `check-syncthing-status.ps1` confirms both machines report full completion. Consider pre-creating a basic empty Obsidian vault shell at the Acer's target sync path first, so Obsidian has something to open immediately while Syncthing populates the real content — user's suggestion, worth doing here rather than starting from a bare empty folder.
*Plugin-doc slice:* whether Calendar is installed or only referenced by old hotkeys, unless already resolved.
### Build 6 - Scheduled Git auto-commit/push + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* nothing from Builds 4-5, can run in parallel.
*Done when:* a script commits and pushes Jarvis on a schedule, survives a forced push-conflict with rebase-and-retry, and a manual dry run shows a clean exit with nothing staged when there's nothing to commit.
### Build 7 - Google Drive consolidation + Desktop sync + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* which Google account survives being named (open item below).
*Done when:* one Drive account carries the main document folders (Mirror on Dell, Stream on Acer) and the Desktop, verified cross-machine.
### Build 8 - `new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl` wiring + plugin-doc pass
*Depends on:* Build 5. `second-brain-claudekit` cloned on the Acer.
*Done when:* both manifest entries run clean on the Acer and flip from `candidate` to `live`. Apply everything learned from the Dell's original Unison setup (the `-fat` flag requirement, the VBS-wrapper console-popup fix, the stale registration script) so the Acer's setup is correct the first time, not rediscovered.
### Build 9 - Wrap-up note + final plugin-doc consolidation
*Depends on:* Builds 4-8 complete.
*Done when:* one summary note records every build's outcome and what's still open; the plugin gap tracker is revisited and updated.
## Codebases: proposed strategy (documented, not yet executed)
Broadening `.stignore` to include the curated `.claude_windows`/`.claude_wsl` mirrors (Build 4, pending the secret-scan gate) also makes this easier: once those sync, per-project Claude config for every codebase already propagates cross-laptop for free through the vault, without touching the code itself. For the code's actual content: no new sync infrastructure, just git discipline. Generalize Build 6's auto-commit script to a short, explicitly named list of actively-dual-laptop repos. Blocking, independent of tooling: `second-brain-claudekit` had 46 unpushed commits and `internship-research-loop` was diverged as of 2026-09-16 — needs cleanup first.

- **The Plan vault**: after Jarvis is fully proven out, roughly two prompts.
- **Codebases**: strategy proposed above, execution not started.
- **C: and D: drive cleanup** on both laptops.

## Why this build exists
Build 1 excluded six folders from .stignore as a conservative default without verifying their actual contents: 20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/.claude_windows, .claude_wsl, 20_Progress/AI/Cursor/.cursor_windows, .cursor_wsl, 20_Progress/AI/Kiro/.kiro_windows, .kiro_wsl. These are meant to be curated, credential-scrubbed mirrors of each machine's Claude/Cursor/Kiro home-directory config (agents, commands, hooks, CLAUDE.md), built by the vault's existing Unison system. The user wants them synced via Syncthing too, on the principle that .stignore should exclude only genuine secrets and settings proven to cause sync conflicts, nothing broader. A manual spot-check this session's planning found only config-shaped files in .claude_windows and .claude_wsl (agents/, commands/, hooks/, CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, Setup.md, Sync-Log.md, skills/), no credentials or session transcripts, and the sampled hook script and CLAUDE.md content were clean. But the vault's own .gitignore comment for these six folders says "AI tool internal state (sessions, history, creds) — never commit," which directly contradicts "credential-scrubbed." That contradiction has not been resolved with real evidence yet. This build resolves it and acts on the result.

## Your job, in order

### 1. Full secret-pattern audit (mandatory gate, do this before touching .stignore at all)
For each of the six folders, run an automated scan for real secret patterns: API key formats (sk-, ghp_, AKIA, generic api_key= assignments with long values), private key headers (-----BEGIN...PRIVATE KEY-----), anything that looks like a session transcript or credential file rather than a config file (.credentials.json, *.jsonl session logs, anything under a "history" or "sessions" path). Do not stop at a keyword grep - also just look at the actual file listing for each folder and flag anything that isn't clearly agents/commands/hooks/skills/CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md/Setup.md/Sync-Log.md-shaped content. For every one of the six folders, reach a clear verdict: CLEAN (safe to sync) or NOT CLEAN (name exactly what was found and why it's risky). Do not average across folders - each of the six gets its own verdict.

### 2. Update .stignore based on the verdicts, not on assumption
Remove only the folders that got a CLEAN verdict in step 1 from .stignore. Any folder that got a NOT CLEAN verdict stays excluded - do not remove it, do not try to fix/scrub it yourself, just report exactly what's in it and why it's staying excluded, and note this as something the user needs to decide on separately. Use the sed-through-Bash workaround (the PreToolUse:Edit hook false-positive-blocks direct edits to .stignore, already documented in the roadmap) - do not fight the hook, do not try to disable or reconfigure it, that's explicitly out of scope for this build.

### 3. Re-review the entire current .stignore line by line
With the secrets-and-proven-churn-only philosophy now explicit, go through every remaining line in .stignore (not just the six from step 1) and write a one-line justification for each: is it a genuine secret, or proven sync-conflict-causing churn (like Build 3's lean-terminal/recent-files-obsidian findings), or neither? If you find a line that doesn't clearly meet either bar, flag it rather than silently removing it - state your reasoning and let the resulting findings note carry the recommendation.

### 4. Check for a new race surface
Unison writes into 20_Progress/AI/Claude Code/<project>/ folders (including the now-synced .claude_windows/.claude_wsl content) on a 15-minute local Scheduled Task, on this same machine that's also running Syncthing. Now that these folders sync cross-laptop too, reason through whether Unison's automated write could land in the same short pre-scan window Build 1/2 found dangerous for a human edit - i.e., could a 15-minute Unison sync collide with an incoming Syncthing change from the Acer in a way that loses data silently, the same class of problem, just with an automated writer instead of a human one. If check-syncthing-status.ps1 (from Build 3, documented in Cross-Laptop Sync - Rollback Procedure.md) can help verify this concretely without needing a second device, use it. Otherwise, reason it through explicitly from what Builds 1-2 already proved and say so.

### 5. Propose a log-hygiene policy
40_Resources/AI/Claude Code/_All-Projects-Sync-Log.md and individual Sync-Log.md files (e.g. second-brain-claudekit's) are unbounded append-only logs, already hundreds of KB to over 1MB. Propose a concrete rotation or size-cap policy (for example: archive entries older than N days into a dated file, or cap active log size and roll over). Implement it only if it can be done entirely within this Jarvis vault's own scripts (e.g. a periodic trim/archive step you add here) - if a real fix requires editing sync-all.sh or other files inside the second-brain-claudekit repo, do NOT edit that repo. Document the recommendation clearly instead and say explicitly that it needs separate authorization, the same way this build is not touching the PreToolUse:Edit hook.

### 6. Strengthen Build 3's findings
40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 3 Findings.md documents the old, more conservative .stignore philosophy. Add a short, clearly-dated addendum section to that note (do not rewrite its existing content) explaining the policy change, what got audited, and the verdicts - so the note's history stays honest about what was believed at the time versus what's known now, rather than silently overwritten.

## Research standard
Every verdict in step 1 must be based on actually reading file listings and sampling real file content this session, not general assumptions about what a "curated mirror" should contain. Cite Syncthing/Unison documentation only where it's directly relevant (e.g. if the race-surface reasoning in step 4 needs it); most of this build is investigation of this vault's actual files, not external research.

## Boundaries
Do not install Syncthing on, or attempt to reach, the Acer or any other machine. Do not pair any new device. Do not create the Jarvis git auto-commit Scheduled Task, edit sync-manifest.json, or touch Google Drive settings (later builds). Do not edit the PreToolUse:Edit hook or any Claude Code settings file. Do not edit the second-brain-claudekit repo. Do not change any Obsidian plugin's actual settings. Continue on the infra/cross-laptop-sync branch, do not merge to master, do not push. If a folder's audit result is ambiguous or you're unsure whether something counts as a secret, ask the user rather than guessing - the whole point of this build is not assuming.

## Claude

Good thing I let those background checks finish — they surfaced something more important than a secrets question. `.claude_wsl` is **1.9GB** (versus `.claude_windows` at 21MB), and the reason is a `skills/gbrain/node_modules/` tree and full test suites living inside what's supposed to be a curated config mirror, plus a `skills/gstack/` tree that duplicates skill definitions across eight different tool-config directories (`.cursor/`, `.factory/`, `.gbrain/`, `.hermes/`, `.kiro/`, `.openclaw/`, `.opencode/`, `.slate/`). The grep did flag several files inside `node_modules` (AWS SDK type definitions, a minified WASM bundle) — those are almost certainly false positives from pattern-matching on bundled/minified code, not real credentials, but that's beside the real point: **this folder contains far more than the curated agents/commands/hooks scope was ever meant to hold**, and syncing 1.9GB of regenerable `node_modules` via Syncthing is its own problem regardless of secrets — the same class of bloat that ruled out Google Drive in the first place.

This changes what Build 4 needs to do: it's not a clean yes/no on secrets per folder, it needs to exclude specific bloat subtrees (`node_modules`, test suites) even where the content is genuinely secret-free. Updating the prompt now before you run it.

Now updating the roadmap's decision note to reflect the real evidence rather than the earlier spot-check framing.

Updating the project memory with this correction, then done.

Build 4's prompt and the roadmap are both updated in place — no need to rerun anything, the same note is still current. The key addition: secrets-clean and sync-worthy are now treated as two separate gates, so Build 4 won't just check for credentials and then sync 1.9GB of `node_modules` because it happened to pass the secret scan. It'll report the actual post-exclusion size per folder so you can see what's really going to move across the wire before it happens.

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap
## One-Line Answer
**Syncthing** carries the live **Jarvis** and **The Plan** vaults between the Dell and the Acer in near-real-time, **Git** keeps Jarvis's version history and GitHub backup on its own schedule (direct-to-`master`), **Google Drive** (Mirror on the Dell, Stream on the Acer) carries plain document folders and the Desktop, and the existing same-machine **Unison** mirror gets two new entries for the Acer's Claude config. `.stignore` excludes only genuine secrets, proven sync-conflict-causing churn, and confirmed regenerable bloat, nothing else — curated config mirrors sync, the dependency trees and IDE caches installed alongside them don't. Codebases stay GitHub-only; Build 4 already caught one real live codebase (`.cursor_wsl/worktrees/.../nkv/`, its own `.git`/`node_modules`/`.env.local`) sitting inside what was assumed to be curated config, so this isn't a hypothetical risk. **Six builds remain** (5-10) before Jarvis is fully done; The Plan then reuses the proven pattern in roughly two prompts.
## Why small builds instead of one setup
This has to run correctly once and never need repair. Builds 1-4 each found something the prior build's stated intention didn't fully cover: a file-lock gap, its cross-device confirmation, machine-specific settings, and — most recently — a "curated" folder that was 99% regenerable bloat plus a live embedded codebase. That pattern (verify, don't assume) is why Build 5 exists before Acer pairing: one more full-vault sweep for anything the six-folder-scoped audits missed (already found one gap: `30_Order/System/excalidraw-mcp/node_modules/` is git-ignored but was never added to `.stignore`), plus finishing the Settings-folder documentation task from the very start of this project that never got done, before calling Jarvis's own side of this actually finished.
## Decisions locked in
Do not re-litigate these in a later build session:
- **Secrets** (`.claude`, `.cursor`, `.codex`, credentials, SSH keys) stay fresh-install-only per machine. Never copied, never synced, by any mechanism. Jarvis is a **public** GitHub repo (`gupta-builds/Jarvis`).
- **Syncthing scope**: the Jarvis and The Plan vaults only. Codebases stay GitHub push/pull only — confirmed not hypothetical, see Build 4's `nkv` worktree finding.
- **`.git/` exclusion**: every Syncthing folder's `.stignore` excludes `.git` completely, non-negotiable.
- **`.stignore` philosophy**: exclude only genuine secrets, proven sync-conflict-causing churn, or confirmed regenerable bloat — not broad categories excluded "to be safe." Applied in Build 4 to the six curated home-dir mirror folders (dropped their synced footprint from ~4.1GB to ~60MB); Build 5 extends the same sweep to the rest of the vault. Full detail: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 4 Findings]].
- **Google Drive**: consolidating from two accounts to one, Dell Mirror mode, Acer Stream mode. User reports this may already be done via a separate effort outside these builds — Build 8 verifies actual current state before assuming either way.
- **Discovery mode**: global discovery + relays.
- **Git commit cadence for Jarvis**: automatic, scheduled.
- **`new-laptop-windows` / `new-laptop-wsl`**: two new entries in the Unison `sync-manifest.json`, writing to distinct per-machine paths, not shared ones (Build 4's race-surface analysis depends on this).
- **Browser sync (Vivaldi)**: out of scope.
- **Obsidian plugin documentation**: use the vault's existing system ([[00 Plugin Reference Index]], [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]]). 15 items resolved across Builds 1-3, do not redo.
- **No second device until Jarvis's own side is fully swept.** Confirmed safety net (Build 2) plus confirmed `.stignore` correctness (Builds 3-5) both gate Acer pairing.
- **Git branch strategy for two laptops stays direct-to-`master`.** Syncthing keeps both working trees converged, so there's rarely anything to diverge; the real risk is two scheduled auto-commit tasks racing to push, handled by Build 7's rebase-and-retry design.
- **Cross-machine sync verification**: Syncthing's own REST API (`check-syncthing-status.ps1`, Build 3), not separate hashing tooling.
- **Claude Code hook friction**: a `PreToolUse:Edit` hook false-positive-blocks direct edits to `.stignore`. Known, not fixed — every build since Build 3 uses the `sed`-through-Bash workaround. Fixing the hook needs the user's explicit go-ahead, not assumed.
## Build order
### Build 0 - Pre-mortem on the design (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
### Build 1 - Syncthing, Dell-only pilot + first plugin-doc pass (COMPLETE, 2026-09-18)
*Outcome:* Syncthing installed, `.stignore` created, Staggered versioning on. File-lock test found a silent-overwrite gap single-machine. Resolved 3 plugin items. [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 1 Findings]]. `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (`78b1ff67`).
### Build 2 - Prove the safety net, harden Syncthing, no second device yet (COMPLETE, 2026-09-19)
*Outcome:* Two local Syncthing instances confirmed real conflict-copy and versioning behavior cross-device; confirmed the silent-loss gap survives, narrowed to the pre-scan window. Autostart Scheduled Task registered and verified. Resolved 3 plugin items. [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 2 Findings]]. `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (`f680af4c`).
### Build 3 - Settings/conflict audit, verification tooling, heavy plugin-doc pass (COMPLETE, 2026-09-19)
*Outcome:* Audited every `.obsidian` setting and plugin `data.json`; added `lean-terminal` and `recent-files-obsidian` data files to `.stignore`. Built and live-tested `check-syncthing-status.ps1` against Syncthing's REST API. Resolved or corrected 9 plugin items. [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 3 Findings]]. `infra/cross-laptop-sync`.
### Build 4 - Broaden `.stignore` to secrets-only, check for new race surfaces, log hygiene (COMPLETE, 2026-09-19)
*Outcome:* Audited all five existing curated mirror folders (`.kiro_windows` never created) — all secrets-clean, but four were 87-99% regenerable bloat (installed IDE extensions, `node_modules`, cached tool output, one embedded live Next.js codebase with its own `.git`). `.stignore` now un-excludes all five real folders with 12 narrow bloat-subtree exclusions in their place: `.claude_windows` 21M (unchanged), `.claude_wsl` ~33M (was 1.9G), `.cursor_windows` ~0.5M (was 987M), `.cursor_wsl` ~3M (was 31M), `.kiro_wsl` ~0.2M (was 1.2G). Two lines flagged as not clearly meeting the secret/churn/bloat bar (`.obsidian/graph.json`, `.obsidian/workspaces.json`) — left as-is for Build 5. Race-surface analysis: no live risk today (only the Dell writes to these paths), but a real one for Build 9 if the Acer's Unison entries ever target shared paths instead of distinct ones. Built and dry-run tested a 7-day log-rotation script, not applied to live logs (would race the 15-minute Scheduled Task mid-write). [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 4 Findings]]. `infra/cross-laptop-sync` (`66314d04`).

## Situation
Builds 1-4 built and hardened Syncthing sync for the Jarvis vault (D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis, public GitHub repo gupta-builds/Jarvis) on this machine (the Dell) alone, proved the conflict/versioning safety net cross-device using two local instances, and audited six named curated-config mirror folders under 20_Progress/AI/{Claude Code,Cursor,Kiro}/ - finding four of them were 87-99% regenerable bloat (node_modules, IDE extensions, cached tool output, one live embedded codebase) rather than curated content, now fixed via 12 path-specific `.stignore` exclusions. ## Your job, in order

### 1. Full-vault sweep for regenerable content beyond the six already-audited folders
Every prior audit scoped to 20_Progress/AI/{Claude Code,Cursor,Kiro}/. Search the entire rest of the vault for the same failure pattern: node_modules, build output (dist/, .next/, target/), test fixtures, IDE extension caches, or any other package-manager-installed content that would be wasteful or wrong to sync. For each one found, exclude it in `.stignore` the same way Build 4 did - a specific path, not a bare directory name (Syncthing's ignore patterns are unanchored by default and a bare name would match everywhere).

Build 4 flagged `.obsidian/graph.json` and `.obsidian/workspaces.json` as not clearly meeting the secret/churn/bloat bar (low git-commit counts, unlike the proven-churn `workspace.json`). ### 3. Check Syncthing's actual live health, not assumed health
Query the running Syncthing instance's REST API directly (`GET /rest/system/error` for any logged errors, `GET /rest/db/status?folder=jarvis` for the folder's current state and error count) rather than assuming everything is fine because prior builds reported success. + ## Settings Confirmed At Obsidian's Factory Default
`app.json` only stores a key when its value differs from the built-in default, so absence is itself a confirmed value, not an undocumented gap. Three sync-relevant settings never appear in this vault's `app.json`, meaning they sit at Obsidian's shipped defaults:
- **New file location** — default (*"Vault folder"*, new notes with no explicit path land at the vault root). This vault never redirects new-note creation through this setting; folder placement instead comes from Templater's `folder_templates` and QuickAdd choices, not from a global default-location override.
- **Default location for new attachments** — default (*"Same folder as current file"*). Never redirected to a single `Attachments/` folder.
- **New link format** — default (*"Shortest path when possible"*), which is why `[[Note Name]]` resolves vault-wide instead of needing a full path, and is part of why [[Jarvis Writing and Formatting]] can rely on plain wikilinks.
This matters for Build 6: the Acer's freshly created basic vault shell will also start at these same Obsidian factory defaults, so none of these three settings can create a values-based conflict when `app.json` syncs over. The only risk is structural — if the Acer's vault has already had notes created before the real `app.json` arrives, its own `app.json` could contain *different* explicit values (not just defaults) that the incoming sync would then overwrite. Build 6 should confirm the Acer's `app.json` is still empty/default before pairing, not assume it.
## Suggestions
Grounded in what's actually configured here, not generic Obsidian advice:
- **Leave `promptDelete` on.** With two AI editors and an MCP server capable of deleting files, this is the one setting that forces a human-visible confirmation inside the Obsidian UI. Turning it off would remove the only UI-level guard against an agent deleting the wrong note.
- **Consider setting `showUnsupportedFiles: true` before the Acer sync, temporarily.** Its default-off state means any non-Markdown file type Obsidian doesn't natively recognize (stray `.docx`, `.zip`, or install artifacts) stays invisible in File Explorer and Quick Switcher — which also means it would stay invisible if a Build 6 audit needed to visually confirm what's about to sync into the Acer's vault. Worth flipping on for the duration of that audit, then back off.
- **`userIgnoreFilters` is not a sync-safety mechanism and shouldn't be treated as one.** All three of its current entries (`50_Archive/`, `excalidraw-mcp/node_modules/`, `60_Claude/00_Inbox/copilot/`) already have their own `.gitignore`/`.stignore` coverage from the cross-laptop sync builds. If a future path only gets added here and not to `.stignore`, it will still sync via Syncthing while staying invisible in Obsidian's own UI — a worse outcome than not excluding it at all, since it hides the problem rather than preventing it. Any future exclusion decision should start in `.gitignore`/`.stignore`, with `userIgnoreFilters` added only as a UI-convenience mirror of that decision, never the other way around.

+ ## Suggestions
- **Before Build 6 pairs the Acer, confirm the AnuPpuccin theme and the two third-party snippets (`myedits.css`, `rainbowfile_colors.css`) actually get installed there, not just synced.** Syncthing will carry the snippet files and `appearance.json`'s theme name, but Obsidian's Community Themes browser is what actually downloads and registers a theme on first use — if the Acer's Obsidian has never installed AnuPpuccin through its own theme browser, a synced `appearance.json` pointing at `cssTheme: AnuPpuccin` with no matching theme folder present could fall back to the default theme silently rather than error. Worth a manual check on first launch, not an assumption.
- **`headerspace.css`, `readingview.css`, and `dashboard.css` are the only three files worth versioning carefully.** If any hand-written CSS work happens on this vault going forward, it belongs in one of these three, not `myedits.css` or `rainbowfile_colors.css` — editing the theme's own extended-settings file directly would get silently overwritten the next time AnuPpuccin ships an update to that schema.
- **Consider a short comment header inside each of the three vault-authored snippets** (`headerspace.css`, `readingview.css`, `dashboard.css`) stating they're hand-written and why, mirroring the distinction this note now documents — makes the difference visible from inside the file itself, not only from this note, for whoever opens Community Themes → Snippets later without vault-history context.

# Core Plugins Hotkeys and Defaults
==One of this vault's eight custom hotkeys is bound to a plugin that isn't installed== — `.obsidian/hotkeys.json` still maps Alt+C to `calendar:show-calendar-view`, but `calendar` never appears in `.obsidian/community-plugins.json`, confirming what [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]] had only guessed at: Calendar is a leftover hotkey, not a leftover plugin.
## What This Note Covers
[[Plugin Inventory and Configuration Map]] lists which community plugins are installed. This note covers the two things neither it nor [[File Handling and Properties]] documents: which of Obsidian's own core (built-in) plugins are actually turned on, and every custom hotkey rebinding, read directly from `.obsidian/core-plugins.json` and `.obsidian/hotkeys.json`.
## Core Plugins — On, Off, and Why It Matters
Read directly from `.obsidian/core-plugins.json`. Off-by-default-and-still-off plugins aren't listed; only the choices worth explaining are:
- **`daily-notes: false`, `templates: false`** — both of Obsidian's own built-in periodic-note and templating systems are off. [[Templates Capture and Periodic Notes]] confirms why: the community Templater plugin replaced both, since it supports the folder-template and per-type logic the core versions can't.
- **`sync: false`** — Obsidian Sync (the official paid subscription sync service) was never turned on. This vault uses Syncthing instead, per [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap]] — not an oversight, the deliberate alternative this entire build sequence exists to implement.
- **`publish: true`** — on, despite [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]] listing "whether Publish is actively used" as an open question. Being enabled only means the core plugin is active; it does not mean a Publish subscription exists or that any note is actually published. Confirms the toggle itself isn't the open question — whether to ever use it for real is.
- **`bases: true`** — on. Obsidian's newer database/table view feature, shipped as a core plugin. Same note as Publish: enabled and available, not the same as "in active use instead of Dataview." [[Dataview and Dashboards]] still owns the vault's actual dashboard logic today.
- **`zk-prefixer: false`, `audio-recorder: false`, `webviewer: false`** — off, consistent with this vault's actual workflow: no Zettelkasten ID-prefixing convention, no voice-note capture, no in-app web browsing.
## Custom Hotkeys
Every rebinding in `.obsidian/hotkeys.json`, eight total:
- `Alt+B` — `backlink:open`
- `Alt+C` — `calendar:show-calendar-view`. **Dead binding** — no `calendar` entry exists in `.obsidian/community-plugins.json`. Pressing Alt+C does nothing. This directly resolves the "installed or only referenced by old hotkeys" question in [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]]: only referenced.
- `Alt+W` — `workspace:close-others`
- `obsidian-excalidraw-plugin:save` — bound to an **empty modifier list**, meaning this hotkey was explicitly cleared by hand at some point, not simply never configured. Excalidraw's save now falls back to whatever default (if any) the plugin itself assigns.
- `Mod+Shift+E` — `file-explorer:open`
- `Alt+V` — `editor:insert-codeblock`
- `Alt+F` — `omnisearch:show-modal`
- `Alt+Q` — `quickadd:runQuickAdd`
- `Alt+M` — `excalibrain:excalibrain-open-hover` — a live binding, consistent with Build 3's finding that Excalibrain is actually installed, correcting an earlier tracker error that assumed it was missing.
## Suggestions
- **Remove the dead `calendar:show-calendar-view` hotkey**, or install the Calendar plugin if periodic-note navigation is actually wanted — right now it's neither working nor documented as intentionally kept. A one-line `data.json` edit, needs explicit permission per this vault's rules, same as any other plugin-settings change.
- **Re-check what `obsidian-excalidraw-plugin:save` currently does with no assigned key.** If Excalidraw has no other default save binding, saves may be relying entirely on Obsidian's own auto-save rather than an explicit action — worth confirming intentional rather than an accidental clear.
- **Decide, don't just note, whether `bases` (core) and `Dataview` (community) are meant to coexist long-term or whether `bases` should eventually replace some Dataview queries** — both being independently "on" without a stated relationship is exactly the kind of ambiguity [[Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification]] exists to close.
- **For Build 6:** `core-plugins.json` and `hotkeys.json` are both small, low-churn config files with no secrets — safe to sync as-is, and worth an explicit line item in Build 6's audit confirming they aren't among the `.stignore` exclusions, since a hotkey mismatch between machines would be a quiet, easy-to-miss annoyance rather than a data-safety issue.

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 6 Prompt
Paste the block below into a fresh Claude Code session opened in this vault (`D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis`). Self-contained — the executing session has no access to anything outside what this prompt, the linked notes, and what the user tells it directly say. Full context: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap]].
```
You are executing Build 6 of a cross-laptop sync project for an Obsidian vault — Acer pairing, the first time a second physical device touches this vault's real data. Read this entire prompt first. Then read, in order: 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Sync/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap.md (full strategy, decisions, build order), 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Sync/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 5 Findings.md, 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Sync/Cross-Laptop Sync - Rollback Procedure.md, and the current D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis\.stignore.

## Situation
Builds 1-5 built and hardened Syncthing sync for the Jarvis vault (D:\Users\_Anant\10_Areas\Documents\Jarvis, public GitHub repo gupta-builds/Jarvis) on the Dell alone: proved the conflict/versioning safety net cross-device using two disposable local instances (Build 2), then swept the entire vault for regenerable content and settled `.stignore` down to secrets/churn/bloat-only exclusions (Builds 3-5). The Dell's own side is sync-ready. Separately, the user has been installing Syncthing on a second laptop (the Acer) and creating a fresh, mostly-empty "basic vault" there as the pairing target.

**Critical difference from every prior test:** Build 2's conflict-copy/versioning proof used a disposable folder that started EMPTY on both sides, then diverged after pairing. The Acer's basic vault is not empty — it already has whatever files a brand-new Obsidian vault creates by default (its own `.obsidian/app.json`, `workspace.json`, `core-plugins.json`, `community-plugins.json`, possibly a placeholder note) before Syncthing ever touches it. Pairing two devices that already have DIFFERENT pre-existing content at the same paths is a genuinely different scenario from anything tested so far, and needs to be understood before it happens, not discovered by watching what breaks.

## Your job, in order

### 1. Get the Acer's actual current file state — do not proceed without it
Ask the user directly for a file listing of the Acer's basic vault if it has not already been provided in this conversation. Specifically request: a recursive file listing of the vault folder, and the actual presence/content of `.obsidian/app.json`, `.obsidian/workspace.json`, `.obsidian/core-plugins.json`, `.obsidian/community-plugins.json`, `.obsidian/hotkeys.json`, and any note files Obsidian auto-created on vault creation. You cannot access the Acer directly — this has to come from the user. Do not guess or assume what a "default" Obsidian vault contains; ask for the real listing.

### 2. Research Syncthing's actual behavior for this specific scenario
Find and cite Syncthing's own documentation or forum guidance for pairing two devices whose shared folder already has non-identical pre-existing content on both sides at the same paths — not the empty-then-diverge scenario Build 2 already proved. Confirm whether Syncthing's first sync treats same-path-different-content files as an immediate conflict-copy situation, or something else. Do not assume Build 2's findings generalize to this case without checking.

### 3. Compare the Acer's real files against the Dell's real vault, path by path
Using the listing from step 1, categorize every relevant file:
- Unique to the Dell — no issue, populates fresh onto the Acer.
- Unique to the Acer's basic vault (Obsidian's own auto-generated defaults, most likely) — decide whether to delete these from the Acer before pairing so the Dell's real content becomes canonical with nothing to collide with, versus leaving them to be evaluated as genuine collisions in step 2's confirmed behavior.
- Present on both sides with different content — the real collision risk. For each one, decide and justify: should the Dell's version simply win (delete the Acer's pre-existing copy before pairing), or is a conflict-copy an acceptable, recoverable outcome per Build 2's proof that the mechanism itself doesn't lose data.

### 4. Decide whether `.stignore` needs any addition based on what you find
If the Acer's fresh Obsidian install reveals any file or setting that's meaningfully device-identity-specific and not already covered by the current `.stignore` (built and audited across Builds 1-5), add it with the same justification standard those builds used — a genuine secret, proven churn, or confirmed bloat, not a guess. Use the sed-through-Bash workaround for the PreToolUse:Edit hook, same as every build since Build 3.

### 5. Produce an exact, step-by-step safe pairing procedure
This is the main deliverable if live pairing isn't possible or approved in this session: a precise procedure (most likely built around clearing the Acer's auto-generated defaults before the first sync, based on what steps 2-3 actually found, not assumed) that another session or the user could follow to pair the two devices without any of the collisions found in step 3 actually happening.

### 6. If the Acer's Syncthing is confirmed reachable and the user explicitly approves, execute the real pairing
Exchange Device IDs, add the Jarvis folder share, and verify using `check-syncthing-status.ps1` (Build 3/5) that both devices reach full completion. Perform one real end-to-end edit test (a change on one device appears on the other) and one deliberate simultaneous-edit test, confirming it reproduces Build 2's proven conflict-copy behavior rather than assuming it does. If the Acer isn't actually ready, stop after step 5's procedure and say so plainly rather than attempting a partial pairing.

### 7. Plugin-documentation slice
Check 40_Resources/Obsidian/Plugins/Plugin Gaps Recommendations and Verification.md directly for whatever remains open after Builds 1-5 — do not assume the list from an earlier build's prompt is still accurate, read the tracker itself. Resolve what you reasonably can, citing official sources, updating the tracker as you go.

## Research standard
Every claim, especially about Syncthing's pre-existing-content pairing behavior, must be checked against current official documentation or the live system this session, not assumed from Build 2's different scenario.

## Boundaries
Do not delete or modify anything on the Acer directly — you have no access to it; any change there happens through the user's own hands, guided by your procedure. Do not create the Jarvis git auto-commit Scheduled Task, edit sync-manifest.json, or touch Google Drive settings (later builds). Do not edit the PreToolUse:Edit hook, any Claude Code settings file, or the second-brain-claudekit repo. Do not change any Obsidian plugin's actual behavioral settings, only document them, except `.stignore` itself if step 4 genuinely calls for it. Continue on the infra/cross-laptop-sync branch, do not merge to master, do not push. If the Acer's file listing reveals something ambiguous or you're unsure whether a collision is safe to let happen, ask the user rather than guessing — this is the first time a second machine touches this vault's real data.

## When finished
Write a new note, 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Sync/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 6 Findings.md, matching the style of the prior findings notes, including the file-by-file comparison from step 3 and the exact pairing procedure from step 5. Update the Build 6 entry in Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap.md to mark it complete (or partially complete, with the procedure documented and live pairing still pending, if step 6 didn't run) with a short factual outcome.

# Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 7 Prompt
**Run this one on the Acer, not the Dell** - the first build in this sequence to do so, since git identity and Drive's Acer-side state genuinely need checking from that machine. Paste the block below into a fresh Claude Code session opened in the Acer's Jarvis vault (Syncthing has it fully synced as of Build 6, so it's the same vault, same notes, different machine). Self-contained. Full context: [[Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap]].
```
You are executing Build 7 of a cross-laptop sync project for an Obsidian vault - the final build closing out Jarvis's side of this entire process before it moves to a sibling vault, The Plan. You are running on the Acer (the new laptop), not the Dell (the old one) - the first build in this sequence to do so. Read this entire prompt first. Then read, in order: 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Sync/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build Roadmap.md (full strategy, decisions, build order), 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Sync/Cross-Laptop Sync - Build 6 Findings.md (how the Dell-Acer pairing actually went, including a real incident and its fix), and 40_Resources/CS/Concepts/New Laptop/Sync/Cross-Laptop Sync - Rollback Procedure.md.

## Situation
Builds 0-6 built, hardened, and proved Syncthing sync for the Jarvis vault (a public GitHub repo, gupta-builds/Jarvis) between the Dell and this Acer - both devices are confirmed live and bidirectionally in sync as of Build 6, verified independently from both sides down to matching SHA-256 file hashes. What's never been built yet: an automatic git commit/push schedule for Jarvis (Syncthing keeps the working tree converged in real time, but git's own version history and GitHub backup still only happen when someone manually commits), a check on whether Google Drive's account consolidation (mentioned by the user as possibly already done through a separate effort) is actually true, and the two new Unison manifest entries (`new-laptop-windows`, `new-laptop-wsl`) that need this exact machine to exist at all.

## Priority order - do not treat these as equally weighted
Do task 1 thoroughly, even if it takes the whole session. Do tasks 2-4 as time and access allow, and report clearly if any of them don't get done rather than rushing them to check a box.

### 1. Scheduled Git auto-commit/push (the primary deliverable)
Write a script - PowerShell, since this runs on Windows - that: pulls with rebase first, commits Jarvis if there's anything to commit (a real diff, not a no-op commit), pushes, and on a push rejection (the other laptop pushed first), pulls with rebase again and retries once before giving up and logging the conflict for a human to resolve manually. Test this specifically: simulate a rejected push (e.g. make a commit look stale by having the remote move ahead some other way) and confirm the rebase-retry actually recovers instead of just failing. A manual dry run with nothing to commit must exit cleanly with nothing staged - do not let the script create empty commits.
Write this script to a path inside the Jarvis vault itself (something like `30_Order/System/claude-workflow/scripts/git-auto-sync.ps1`, matching this vault's existing script-location convention - check what's already there first) rather than somewhere Acer-only. Since the vault is Syncthing-synced, the script text itself reaches the Dell automatically the next time it syncs - the Dell does not need its own separate build for this, only its own Scheduled Task registered locally pointing at the same script once it arrives. Document that one remaining step clearly in your findings note so it's obvious what's still needed on the Dell.
Register a Scheduled Task on this Acer that runs the script on an interval (match the existing `ClaudeKit-Sync-All` cadence of 15 minutes unless you find a real reason not to - check that task's own registration for the pattern to follow, including whatever fixed a console-popup/exit-code bug in that task's own history, documented in [[Sync - Unison]]). Verify the task is registered correctly and actually fires once before calling this done.

### 2. Check Google Drive's real current state
The user has said this may already be consolidated to one account with Mirror mode on the Dell and Stream mode on the Acer, done through a separate effort outside these numbered builds.
